\|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\leq C
,
Lemma

A.2
a) (which requires flatness around the origin of the condensate)
and that we can choose
m
large enough (for fixed
n,s>0
), such that
\Lambda^{-sm}\leq\Lambda^{-2n(\frac{1}{3}-s)}
.

Since
|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}
is missing a flatness condition around the origin, the estimate of (
4.8
) is considerably more delicate. However, using the estimate of the localized condensate
\Theta_{\Lambda}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
from Appendix
B.2
we can complete the argument.
We again use
Lemma

A.2
b) to move
\Theta_{\Lambda}
inside the convolution to conclude

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\sqrt{\rho}W\ast(|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\rho}C\|\Theta_{\Lambda}(|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2})\|_{1\wedge 2\wedge\infty}

\displaystyle\quad+C\sqrt{\rho}\Lambda^{-ms}\|\,|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}\|_{1\wedge 2\wedge\infty}

Note that the norm
\|\,.\,\|_{1\wedge 2\wedge\infty}
is defined in
A.1
. We use
B.8
, for
(\beta=0,\tilde{k}=k-1)
, and
B.4
to get that
\forall m\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\sqrt{\rho}W\ast(|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\rho}C\Big\{\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{6}-\frac{k}{3}-ks}+\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}s-2n(1/3-s)}\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad+C\sqrt{\rho}\Lambda^{-\left(\frac{1}{6}+ms\right)}\,.

(4.10)

Since
s>0
, we can choose
m
in (
4.10
) large enough such that
\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{6}}\Lambda^{-ms}\leq\Lambda^{-s/2-2n(1/3-s)}
.
With this choice,
4.3
follows directly from (
4.7
), (
4.8
), (
4.9
) and (
4.10
).
∎

4.2
Tracer Localization

In this section we explain the idea behind the tracer localization in both position and momentum space in the dynamics generated by
H^{\rm BF}_{\Lambda}(t)
, given in
Theorem

2.14
. Its proof can be found at the end of this section.

Our goal is to show that the tracer remains confined within a region of
{\mathcal{O}}(1)
over timescales of
{\mathcal{O}}(1)
.
For this, we have to control two contributions to its dynamics, one coming from the condensate
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
and one from the excitations.

\bullet
Condensate Contribution.

This is minor, as the largest contribution that arises from its interaction with the tracer,
\sqrt{\rho}W\ast|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}(x)
, has already been extracted from the dynamics.

\bullet
Excitation Contribution.

We will show that the tracer gains at most
\mathcal{O}(1)
energy from its interactions with the excitations. This involves proving two key points:

1. The Number of Effective Interactions.

The tracer effectively interacts with only
\mathcal{O}(1)
many excitations. Heuristically, this follows from the finite
{\mathcal{O}}(1)
-range of the tracer-boson interaction potential
W
, together with the assumption that the globally present excitations of essential order
\Lambda
(see
3.4
) are roughly evenly distributed in the gas.

2. The Energy Gain per Excitation.

The tracer particle gains at most
\mathcal{O}(1)
energy from each excitation it interacts with, as controlled by the interaction prefactor in
H^{\rm BF}_{\Lambda}
. Rescaling the term
\frac{1}{\rho^{r}}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{N}W(x-y_{i})
in
H_{\rho}
yields an interaction
\rho^{1/2-r}A(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda})
in
H^{\rm BF}_{\Lambda}
. To ensure localization, this energy gain must remain
\mathcal{O}(1)
, requiring
r\geq 1/2
. For
r<1/2
, the interaction is too strong and localization may fail.

Number of Effectively Interacting Excitations with the Tracer.

To establish localization of the tracer, we derive bounds on the moments of its position operator,
\langle\psi^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,t},|x|^{2M}\psi^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,t}\rangle
, by using Grönwall-type arguments. For a simpler understanding, consider
\langle\psi^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,t},x_{i}\psi^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,t}\rangle
.
Its time derivative involves the commutator
[x_{i},H^{\rm BF}_{\Lambda}]=2\partial_{x_{i}}
, requiring bounds on
\partial_{x_{i}}
. Applying the same strategy to
\partial_{x_{i}}
, we encounter the commutator
[\partial_{x_{i}},H^{\rm BF}_{\Lambda}]=a(\partial_{x_{i}}Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda})+a^{*}(\partial_{x_{i}}Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda})\leq C({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{1/2}
. According to
3.4
,
({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{1/2}
is expected to scale as
\Lambda^{1/2}
, which does not suffice to conclude localization of
x_{i}
to an
{\mathcal{O}}(1)
region.

To improve the above estimate, we conjugate
H^{\rm BF}_{\Lambda}(t)
with the propagator of the Bogoliubov Hamiltonian,
U_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{Bog}}:=U^{\mathrm{Bog}}_{\Lambda}(t,0)=U_{{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}}
, which is itself a Bogoliubov transformation (see (
1.21
)).
By the conjugation with
U_{{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}}
we extract the excitations that are effectively non-interacting with the tracer. The transformed Hamiltonian is

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda}(t)={}

\displaystyle I\otimes(U^{\rm Bog}_{\Lambda,t})^{*}H^{\rm BF}_{\Lambda}(t)I\otimes U^{\rm Bog}_{\Lambda,t}+{\mathrm{i}}\left(I\otimes\partial_{t}U^{\rm Bog}_{\Lambda,t}\right)^{*}I\otimes U^{\rm Bog}_{\Lambda,t}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{\Delta_{x}}{2m}+A\left(\big({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\big)\right)\,.

(4.11)

In the transformed dynamics we get in the Grönwall argument for the excitation number operator that

A\left(\big({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\big)\right)\leq C\|({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}\|_{\rm op}({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{1/2}\,.

And hence due to
\|{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\rm op}\leq C
(see (
C.73
)),
({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}=S{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{*}S
and Grönwall:

\displaystyle{\left\langle\widetilde{\psi}_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}},({\mathcal{N}}+1)\widetilde{\psi}_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}}\right\rangle}\leq C\,,\quad t\in[-T,T]

(4.12)

if we assume
{\left\langle\widetilde{\psi}_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{BF}},({\mathcal{N}}+1)\widetilde{\psi}_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{BF}}\right\rangle}\leq C
.
Although we have extracted a global number of excitations growing with the volume
\Lambda
with the Bogoliubov transformation
U_{\Lambda,t}^{\rm Bog}
coming from
H^{\rm Bog}_{\Lambda}(t)
(see
3.4
), the interaction term with the tracer particle has changed only by
{\mathcal{O}}(1)
due to
\|{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\rm op}\leq C
. This indicates that the tracer effectively interacts with only
{\mathcal{O}}(1)
many excitations.
This way
\|{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\rm op}
controls the local quantity of effective interacting excitations with the tracer.

In the transformed dynamics, the Grönwall estimate becomes:

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda}:x_{i}

\displaystyle\overset{-\Delta_{x}}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{i}}

\displaystyle\overset{A(({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}))}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\mathclap{\underset{\eqref{eq:Ex-Number-Bound-Transformed-Dynamics}}{\overset{\text{Estimate}}{\longrightarrow}}}\quad

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1)

\displaystyle\,,

ensuring localization.
Since
I\otimes U_{\Lambda,t}^{\rm Bog}
commutes with
x\otimes I
, bounds on tracer position in the transformed system
\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda}
carry over to the original dynamics.

The bound on the excitation number in the transformed dynamics indicates that indeed the excitations are evenly distributed in the gas as claimed in our heuristic argument.

Generalization of the Initial Conditions.

In addition to the Bogoliubov transformation
U^{\rm Bog}_{\Lambda,t}
, we can apply a time-independent Bogoliubov transformation,
U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}
.
This allows us to follow a similar argument as above while generalizing the initial state in
Theorem

2.14
from
\psi^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,0}=\widetilde{\psi}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,0}
to
\psi^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,0}=I\otimes U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}\widetilde{\psi}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,0}
(see (
4.14
) below), where we only need to assume control of the excitation number on the transformed initial state, as in
2.10
.

The resulting transformed Hamiltonian takes the form:

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}(t)

\displaystyle=U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}\left((U^{\rm Bog}_{\Lambda,t})^{*}H^{\rm BF}_{\Lambda}(t)U^{\rm Bog}_{\Lambda,t}+{\mathrm{i}}\left(\partial_{t}U^{\rm Bog}_{\Lambda,t}\right)^{*}U^{\rm Bog}_{\Lambda,t}\right)U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Delta_{x}}{2m}+A\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda})\right)\,.

(4.13)

The dynamics of the original state
\psi_{t}
under
H^{\rm BF}_{\Lambda}(t)
are equivalent to the transformed dynamics:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{i}}\partial_{t}\psi_{t}={}

\displaystyle H^{\rm BF}_{\Lambda}(t)\psi_{t}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad{\mathrm{i}}\partial_{t}U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}(U^{\rm Bog}_{\Lambda,t})^{*}\psi_{t}=\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}(t)U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}(U^{\rm Bog}_{\Lambda,t})^{*}\psi_{t}\,.

(4.14)

Technical Implementation.

For a rigorous version of the Grönwall estimate we use
[
27
, Theorem 8]
, which was adapted to our setting in
[
41
, Theorem D.1.1]
.
Specifically, we apply
[
27
, Theorem 8]
to the time-dependent quadratic form generated by
\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}(t)
with comparison operator
A:=B:=h_{\rm oc}^{M}
,
M\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
. Here,
h_{\rm oc}^{M}=(-\Delta_{x}+x^{2}+({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{2})^{M}
is the well studied harmonic oscillator in the tracer position
x
, which controls
x^{2M},(-\Delta_{x})^{M}
and
({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{2M}
.
The conditions of the theorem are verified in
Lemma

4.5
below.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
M\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
. Assume that the potentials
V
and
W
satisfy
2.4
max{M,2}
.

i)

(Condensate condition) For all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
, let
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
be the solution of the Hartree equation (
1.5
). Assume that its initial data
\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}
varies on the scale
\Lambda^{1/3}
, that is,

2.6
and
2.7
4
. Furthermore, we require that
\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}
is flat around the origin, namely
2.8
2,s
,
0<s<1/3
, and assume that
\exists\eta\in H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
,
\delta
,
C>0
such that
\forall\Lambda\geq 1

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}(\Lambda^{1/3}\,.\,)-\eta\|_{2}\leq C\Lambda^{-\delta}\,.

(4.15)

ii)

(Bogoliubov map) For all densities
\rho\geq 1
and volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
let
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{L}}(L^{2}\oplus JL^{2})
be a unitarily implementable Bogoliubov map such that
\exists C>0
and
0<\epsilon\leq 1/4\min\{\delta,\,s,\,3/2(1/3-s),\,1/6\}
with
\forall\Lambda,\rho\geq 1

\displaystyle\|\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}{\mathcal{T}}^{-1}(\tau\oplus J\tau J^{*})\|_{{\mathcal{L}}(L^{2}\oplus JL^{2})}\leq C\,,\quad\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{{\mathcal{L}}(L^{2}\oplus JL^{2})}\leq C\Lambda^{\epsilon}\,.

Then for all
T\geq 0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all densities
\rho\geq 1
, volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
and
-T\leq t\leq T
we have

a)

That

\displaystyle Ch_{\rm oc}^{M}\geq\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}(t)\geq-Ch_{\rm oc}^{M}

(4.16)

and the operator
h_{\rm oc}^{M}
bounds the commutator of
h_{\rm oc}^{M}
with
\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}(t)
, meaning

\displaystyle\mp 2\mathrm{Im}\,{\left\langle\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}(t)\psi,h_{\rm oc}^{M}\psi\right\rangle}\leq C{\left\langle\psi,h_{\rm oc}^{M}\psi\right\rangle}\,,\quad\forall\psi\in D(h_{\rm oc}^{M})\,.

(4.17)

The time derivative of
\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}(t)
is bounded by
h_{\rm oc}^{M}
, meaning
\forall\psi\in Q(h_{\rm oc}^{M})
:
\big(t\mapsto\langle\psi,\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}(t)\psi\rangle\big)\in C^{1}({\mathbb{R}},\mathbb{R})
and

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{d}{dt}\big\langle\psi,\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}(t)\psi\big\rangle\Big|\leq C{\left\langle\psi,h_{\rm oc}^{M}\psi\right\rangle}\,.

(4.18)

The proof of
Lemma

4.5
can be found in Appendix
C.3
.
Note that the bounds obtained in
Lemma

4.5
are uniform in
\Lambda
and
\rho
, which allows us, together with the validity of
[
27
, Theorem 8]
, to prove
Theorem

2.14
and thereby establish tracer localization.

Proof of
Theorem

2.14
.

Due to
2.10
{}_{\psi_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{BF}},M,\epsilon}
there exists a unitarily implementable Bogoliubov map
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{L}}(L^{2}\oplus JL^{2})
such that we have
\psi_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{BF}}\in I\otimes U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}Q(h_{\rm oc}^{M})\cap Q({\mathrm{d}}\Gamma(1-\Delta))\subset Q(h_{\rm oc})\cap Q({\mathrm{d}}\Gamma(1-\Delta))
with

\displaystyle{\left\langle\psi_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{BF}},U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}h_{\rm oc}^{M}U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}\psi_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{BF}}\right\rangle}\leq C\,.

(4.19)

We aim to estimate the dynamics
\psi_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}}
. To this end, we apply two Bogoliubov transformations and define the transformed state by
\widetilde{\psi}_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}}=I\otimes U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}U^{\rm Bog}_{\Lambda}(t,0)^{*}\psi_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}}
with initial data
\widetilde{\psi}_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{BF}}:=I\otimes U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}\psi_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{BF}}
, where
U^{\rm Bog}_{\Lambda}(t,0)
denotes the propagator of the Bogoliubov Hamiltonian as discussed above.
In
\widetilde{\psi}_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}}
, we have extracted the dominant contribution to the excitation number coming from
U^{\mathrm{Bog}}_{\Lambda}(t,0)
. We can now apply the Grönwall-type estimate
[
27
, Theorem 8]
to the transformed state
\widetilde{\psi}_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}}
to control the growth of
h_{\mathrm{oc}}^{M}
under the dynamics. Specifically, for
T\geq 0
there exists a
C>0
such that for all densities
\rho\geq 1
, volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
and
-T\leq t\leq T

\displaystyle\big\langle\widetilde{\psi}_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}},h_{\rm oc}^{M}\widetilde{\psi}_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}}\big\rangle\leq C\big\langle\widetilde{\psi}_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{BF}},h_{\rm oc}^{M}\widetilde{\psi}_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{BF}}\big\rangle\,.

(4.20)

The verification of the conditions of
[
27
, Theorem 8]
is carried out in
Lemma

4.5
. To transfer the estimate (
4.20
) back to the original dynamics
\psi_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}}
, we note that the operator
I\otimes U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}U^{\mathrm{Bog}}_{\Lambda}(t,0)^{*}
commutes with
x^{2M}\otimes I
and
(-\Delta_{x})^{M}\otimes I
, and that
(-\Delta_{x})^{M}+x^{2M}\leq h_{\rm oc}^{M}
. Thus, using (
4.19
), we obtain

\displaystyle{\left\langle\psi_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}},\big(x^{2M}+(-\Delta_{x})^{M}\big)\psi_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}}\right\rangle}\leq C{\left\langle\psi_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{BF}},U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}}^{*}h_{\rm oc}^{M}U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}\psi_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{BF}}\right\rangle}\leq C\,,

which proves the claim.
∎

5
Infinite-Volume Approximation

In this section, we establish the effective description of the dynamics through the infinite-volume Bogoliubov-Fröhlich Hamiltonian
H^{\rm BF}_{\infty}
.
To this end, we first motivate the infinite-volume limit, which leads to the general result stated in
Theorem

5.1
.

To derive the infinite-volume limit description of the effective dynamics,
we restrict ourselves to the case where the interaction potentials
V
and
W
are independent of
\rho
and
\Lambda
.

As in the tracer localization argument of
Section

4.2
, we have to extract the Bogoliubov Hamiltonian
H^{\mathrm{Bog}}_{\Lambda}(t)
from
H^{\rm BF}_{\Lambda}(t)
, since it generates a divergent number of excitations that grows with the volume (see
3.4
). This is done by conjugating with the propagator
U^{\rm Bog}_{\Lambda,t}=U_{{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}}
of the Bogoliubov Hamiltonian.
Although in general
U_{{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}}
does not converge as
\Lambda\to\infty
, the corresponding Bogoliubov map
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}
admits a well-defined limit, introduced later in (
5.5
).

Moreover, as in
Theorem

2.12
, we consider initial data for the excitation dynamics of the form
\psi_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{BF}}=U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}\psi^{\Lambda}
, with
{\left\langle\psi^{\Lambda},({\mathcal{N}}+1)\psi^{\Lambda}\right\rangle}\leq C
. Here the Bogoliubov transformation
U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}
can create order
\Lambda^{1+2\epsilon}
many excitations. Accordingly, we conjugate the Hamiltonian with
U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}
in order to extract these contributions from the initial state to the dynamics.

The resulting Hamiltonian
\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}
relevant in the infinite-volume limit is given in (
4.13
), precisely

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}=-\frac{\Delta_{x}}{2m}+A\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\right)\right)\,.

(5.1)

In order to define the infinite-volume limit of the Hamiltonian, we introduce the corresponding limits of all quantities appearing in
\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}
, while retaining their precise definitions for each theorem in which they are used.

For the initial data we assume that for
\Lambda\to\infty
the Bogoliubov map
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}
admits a limit
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}
(possibly unbounded), which satisfies the symmetry condition
{\mathcal{J}}{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}{\mathcal{J}}={\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}
with
{\mathcal{J}}=\begin{pmatrix}&J^{*}\\
J&\end{pmatrix}
.
For the initial condensate, scaled to order one, we assume that

\displaystyle\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}(\Lambda^{1/3}\,.\,)\to\eta

(5.2)

in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
. In this setting, we define the limit of the constant
\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}
, appearing in the Hartree equation (
1.5
), as

\displaystyle\mu^{\infty}=\frac{1}{2}\int|\eta|^{4}\int V\,.

(5.3)

Further we make use of the flatness of the condensate around the origin in order to neglect the Laplacian in the Hartree equation (
1.5
), leading us to the limit

\displaystyle\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\to{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)}\,.

(5.4)

To define
\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}
in the infinite-volume limit, we construct a candidate
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}
for the limit of
{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)S}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}
, by taking the limit of its generator.
Thus, we define
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}
as the solution of the differential equation

\displaystyle{\mathrm{i}}\partial_{t}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}={\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}(t){\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}\,,\quad{\mathcal{V}}_{0}^{\infty}=I\,,

(5.5)

with generator

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}=\begin{pmatrix}-\frac{\Delta}{2}+K_{1}^{\infty}&-K_{2}^{\infty}\\
(K_{2}^{\infty})^{*}&-J\big(-\frac{\Delta}{2}+K_{1}^{\infty}\big)J^{*}\end{pmatrix}\,,

(5.6)

where
K_{1}^{\infty}
is the limit of
K_{1}^{\Lambda}(t)
and
K_{2}^{\infty}
the limit of
{\mathrm{e}}^{2{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)}K_{2}^{\Lambda}(t)
, given by

\displaystyle K_{1}^{\infty}\psi

\displaystyle=V\ast\psi\,,

(5.7)

\displaystyle K_{2}^{\infty}(t)J\psi

\displaystyle=V\ast\psi^{*}\,,

(5.8)

for
\psi\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
.
Equation (
5.5
) has a unique global solution, which can be proved similarly to
[
7
, Lemma 4.8]
or
[
41
, Lemma D.2.6]
. Rigorous convergence results on
\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}
,
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
and
K_{i}^{\Lambda}(t)
are collected in the appendix (see
Lemma

C.3
). There it is also shown that
{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)S}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}\to{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}
strongly in
L^{2}\oplus JL^{2}
as
\Lambda\to\infty
(see
C.7
).

Motivated by the limits established above, we define the infinite-volume Hamiltonian:

\displaystyle H^{\infty}(t):=-\frac{\Delta_{x}}{2m}+A\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}(W_{x}\oplus JW_{x})\right)\,.

(5.9)

Since
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}
and
({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}
are of the form
\begin{pmatrix}a&J^{*}bJ\\
b&JaJ^{*}\end{pmatrix}
we know that
H^{\infty}(t)
is symmetric. Moreover, by
[
27
, Theorem 8]
,
H^{\infty}(t)
generates a well-defined dynamics, such that the differential equation

{\mathrm{i}}\partial_{t}\psi_{t}^{\infty}={}H^{\infty}(t)\psi_{t}^{\infty}
,
\psi_{t=0}^{\infty}=\psi_{0}^{\infty}
has in a weak sense the unique global solution given by

\psi_{t}^{\infty}={}U^{\infty}(t,0)\psi_{0}^{\infty}
,
\forall\psi_{0}^{\infty}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},{\mathcal{F}}(L^{2})),

where
U^{\infty}_{t}:=U^{\infty}(t,0)
is the unitary propagator of
H^{\infty}(t)
.
The infinite-volume Hamiltonian
H^{\infty}(t)
admits a considerably simpler time evolution than
\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}(t)
. In fact, the condensate evolution governed by the Hartree equation reduces to a constant phase factor, while the time evolution of
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}
can be made explicit (see (
5.12
) below).

Diagonalization of
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}
.

The diagonalization of the translation-invariant Bogoliubov map
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}
is well known in the literature, see for example
[
11
]
.
To fix notation we repeat the results here.
In Fourier representation
{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}
takes the form

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}}=({\mathcal{F}}\oplus J{\mathcal{F}}J^{*}){\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\oplus J{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}J^{*})

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&J{\mathcal{C}}R\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}c&-b\\
b&-c\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&R{\mathcal{C}}J^{*}\end{pmatrix}\,,

(5.10)

where
c(p)=\frac{p^{2}}{2}+(2\pi)^{3/2}\widehat{V}(p)
,
b(p)=(2\pi)^{3/2}\widehat{V}(p)
,
{\mathcal{C}}\psi=\psi^{*}
and
R\psi(p)=\psi(-p)
. If
\widehat{V}\geq 0
, we can explicitly diagonalize
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}}
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}}{\mathcal{T}}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}\omega&0\\
0&-J\omega J^{*}\end{pmatrix}\,,

(5.11)

where
\omega(p)=\sqrt{c^{2}-b^{2}}=\sqrt{p^{4}/4+p^{2}\widehat{V}(p)(2\pi)^{3/2}}
and the Bogoliubov map
{\mathcal{T}}
is defined in (
1.24
).
Since
\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}}
is generated by
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}}
(see (
5.5
)), we find

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}}={\mathcal{T}}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\omega}&0\\
0&J{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\omega}J^{*}\end{pmatrix}{\mathcal{T}}

(5.12)

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}L(t)&M(t)^{*}{\mathcal{C}}RJ^{*}\\
J{\mathcal{C}}RM(t)&JL(t)J^{*}\end{pmatrix}

(5.13)

with

\displaystyle L(t)

\displaystyle=\cos(\omega t)-{\mathrm{i}}\frac{c}{\omega}\sin(\omega t)\,,\quad M(t)=-{\mathrm{i}}\frac{b}{\omega}\sin(\omega t)\,.

(5.14)

Convergence to the Infinite Volume Dynamics.

We are now able to prove the convergence of the dynamics generated by the transformed microscopic dynamics to the one generated by
H^{\infty}(t)
.

Theorem 5.1
.

For given
\alpha,s\in(0,1/3)
choose
\Lambda=\rho^{\alpha}\geq 1
,
n,k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
large enough and
\epsilon>0
small enough (as in
2.13
). Assume that the potentials
V
and
W
satisfy
2.4
n
.
For the initial condensate
\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}
we impose the conditions of
Theorem

2.12
.

Furthermore, assume that there exists a family of unitarily implementable Bogoliubov maps
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}
that satisfies
2.1
with growth rate
\epsilon
and limiting operator
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}
.

Let
\psi_{0}^{\infty}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},{\mathcal{F}}(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})))
and assume that there exist a family of states
\psi_{0}^{\Lambda}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},L^{2}_{\rm s}({\mathbb{R}}^{3N}))
with
U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}U_{0}^{\Lambda}\psi_{0}^{\Lambda}\to\psi_{0}^{\infty}
in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},{\mathcal{F}}(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
as
\rho^{\alpha}=\Lambda\to\infty
, where
U_{t}^{\Lambda}
denotes the excitation map.
Then, for all times
T\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[-T,T]}\|{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}\nu_{t}^{\Lambda}}U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}U_{{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}}^{*}U_{t}^{\Lambda}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}tH_{\rho}}\psi_{0}^{\Lambda}-U_{t}^{\infty}\psi_{0}^{\infty}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},{\mathcal{F}}(L^{2}))}\to 0

as
\rho^{\alpha}=\Lambda\to\infty
, where
U_{t}^{\infty}
denotes the unitary propagator of
H^{\infty}(t)
,
U_{{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}}
the propagator of
H^{\rm Bog}_{\Lambda}(t)
,
\nu_{t}^{\Lambda}=\int^{t}_{0}(\rho^{1/2}\int W-\mu_{s}^{\Lambda})ds\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}\in{\mathbb{R}}
is given by (
1.7
).

Proof of
Theorem

5.1
.

We first restrict ourselves to the case of regular initial data for the infinite-volume dynamics. Let
\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}\in Q((-\Delta_{x}+x^{2}+({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{2})^{2n(1+4\alpha(1+2\epsilon))})\cap Q({\mathrm{d}}\Gamma(-\Delta))
, where we choose
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
as in
Theorem

2.12
. Let
\epsilon>0
such that it satisfies (
2.19
) and we have (
2.1
).
For the initial data of the microscopic dynamics we choose a specific sequence

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\Lambda}:=(U_{0}^{\Lambda})^{*}\mathbbm{1}^{\leq N}U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}\Gamma(Q_{0}^{\Lambda})\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}\,,

(5.15)

where
\mathbbm{1}^{\leq N}
is the projection to the truncated Fock space with at most
N=\lceil\rho\Lambda\rceil
particles.
Note that, due to the conditions imposed on
U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}
, we have
U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}\Gamma(Q_{0}^{\Lambda})\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}\in Q({\mathrm{d}}\Gamma(-\Delta+1))\cap L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},{\mathcal{F}}(\{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\}^{\perp}))
. Thus the action of
(U_{0}^{\Lambda})^{*}
in the definition of
\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\Lambda}
is well defined, and
\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\Lambda}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},H^{1}_{\rm s}({\mathbb{R}}^{3N}))
.

Applying
Theorem

2.12
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}U_{{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}}^{*}\left({\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}\int^{t}_{0}(\rho^{1/2}\int W-\mu_{s}^{\Lambda})ds}U_{t}^{\Lambda}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}tH_{\rho}}\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\Lambda}-\psi_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}}\right)\right\|\leq C\rho^{\frac{3(1+2\epsilon)\alpha-1}{2}}\,,

(5.16)

where
\psi_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}}
is the solution of the effective Bogoliubov-Fröhlich dynamics with initial datum
\psi_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{BF}}=U_{0}^{\Lambda}\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\Lambda}
(see
1.1
). The conditions of
Theorem

2.12
apply, since
U_{0}^{\Lambda}\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\Lambda}
satisfies
2.10
2n
, especially (
2.13
), as we now show. To obtain the bound (
2.14
) on
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}
required in
2.10
we use the convergence (
2.2
) together with the uniform boundedness principle.
The tracer localization (
2.13
) follows from combining the bound
\mathbbm{1}^{>N}\leq({\mathcal{N}}/N)^{m}
for all
m\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
, the estimate
U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{m}U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}\leq C\Lambda^{m}({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{m}
(see
[
7
, Lemma 4.4]
), the strong convergence
\Gamma(Q_{0}^{\Lambda})\to 1
(see
C.4
), and the fact that
\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}\in Q((-\Delta_{x}+x^{2}+({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{2})^{2n(1+4\alpha(1+2\epsilon))})
. Moreover, in the same way, one shows that

\displaystyle U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}U_{0}^{\Lambda}\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\Lambda}\to\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}\,,\quad\rho^{\alpha}=\Lambda\to\infty\,.

(5.17)

It remains to prove that

\displaystyle\left\|U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}U_{{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}}^{*}\psi_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}}-U^{\infty}_{t}\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},{\mathcal{F}})}\to 0\,,

(5.18)

which would yield the claim for initial data
\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}
and
\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\Lambda}
.
To simplify the notation we set
\phi_{t}^{\Lambda}=U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}U_{{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}}^{*}\psi_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}}
. Then

\displaystyle\pm\partial_{t}\left\|\phi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\psi_{t}^{\infty}\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},{\mathcal{F}})}^{2}=\pm 2\mathrm{Im}{\left\langle\phi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\psi_{t}^{\infty},\big(\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}(t)-H^{\infty}(t)\big)\phi_{t}^{\Lambda}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\phi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\psi_{t}^{\infty}\|\Big(\sup_{x}(1+x^{2})^{-1/2}\left\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}F^{\Lambda}_{x}(t)-{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}F^{\infty}_{x}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}\oplus JL^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\times\|(1+x^{2})^{1/2}({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{1/2}\phi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|\,,

(5.19)

where we introduced
F^{\Lambda}_{x}(t)=({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda})
and
F^{\infty}_{x}(t)=({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}(W_{x}\oplus JW_{x})
, which we localized with
(1+x^{2})^{-1/2}
around the origin. The term
\|(1+x^{2})^{1/2}({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{1/2}\phi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|
is controlled by our tracer localization argument, discussed in
Section

4.2
. In fact with (
4.20
) and
2.10
{}_{\psi_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{BF}},M=2}
, we get

\displaystyle\|(1+x^{2})^{1/2}({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{1/2}\phi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|\leq C\|h_{\rm oc}U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}\psi_{\Lambda,0}^{\mathrm{BF}}\|\leq C\,.

(5.20)

We now show that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+x^{2})^{-1/2}\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}F^{\Lambda}_{x}(t)-{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}F^{\infty}_{x}(t)\|_{L^{2}\oplus JL^{2}}\to 0\,.

(5.21)

Let
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
fixed. Then we use that
\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{\rm op}\leq C\Lambda^{\epsilon}
to get

\displaystyle(1+x^{2})^{-1/2}\big\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}F^{\Lambda}_{x}(t)-{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}F^{\infty}_{x}(t)\big\|

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{\epsilon}(1+x^{2})^{-1/2}\big\|F^{\Lambda}_{x}(t)-F^{\infty}_{x}(t)\big\|

(5.22)

\displaystyle\quad+(1+x^{2})^{-1/2}\big\|\big({\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}-{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}\big)({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}(W_{x}\oplus JW_{x})\big\|\,.

(5.23)

We conclude the convergence (
5.22
)
\to 0
as
\Lambda\to\infty
, from
Lemma

C.8
. The convergence of (
5.23
) is proven below.
We use the diagonalization of
\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}}
in (
5.12
) to obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:aux-eq-109}=(1+x^{2})^{-1/2}\Big\|\big(\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}-\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}}\big){\mathcal{T}}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\omega}&0\\
0&J{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\omega}J^{*}\end{pmatrix}{\mathcal{T}}\big(\widehat{W_{x}}\oplus J\widehat{W_{x}}\big)\Big\|

\displaystyle=(1+x^{2})^{-1/2}\Big\|\big(\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}-\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}}\big){\mathcal{T}}^{-1}\big({\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\omega}\tau\widehat{W_{x}}\oplus J{\mathcal{C}}Re^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\omega}\tau\widehat{W_{x}}\big)\Big\|\,.

(5.24)

Now we use
\widehat{W_{x}}(p)=e^{{\mathrm{i}}px}\widehat{W}(p)
to get, since
e^{{\mathrm{i}}px}
is unitary,

(
5.24
)

\displaystyle\leq(1+x^{2})^{-1/2}\Big\|\left[\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}-\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}},{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}px}\oplus J{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}px}J^{*}\right]{\mathcal{T}}^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\times\big({\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\omega}\tau\widehat{W_{x}}\oplus J{\mathcal{C}}R{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\omega}\tau\widehat{W_{x}}\big)\Big\|

(5.25)

\displaystyle\quad+\Big\|\big(\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}-\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}}\big){\mathcal{T}}^{-1}\big({\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\omega}\tau\widehat{W}\oplus J{\mathcal{C}}Re^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\omega}\tau\widehat{W}\big)\Big\|\,,

(5.26)

where (
5.25
) and (
5.26
) converge to zero due to convergence of the commutator of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}-{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}
with translations and the convergence of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}\to{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}
(see
2.1
).
And thus we have the full convergence (
5.21
).

We conclude using Grönwall from (
5.19
) and (
5.20
) that

\displaystyle\left\|\phi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\psi_{t}^{\infty}\right\|\leq\left\|\phi_{0}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+x^{2})^{-1/2}\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}F^{\Lambda}_{x}(s)-{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}F^{\infty}_{x}(s)\|_{L^{2}\oplus JL^{2}}\,ds\,.

(5.27)

Then (
5.18
) follows from (
5.21
), dominated convergence, and
\phi_{0}^{\Lambda}\to\tilde{\psi}^{\infty}_{0}
in (
5.17
). Indeed, for
s\in[0,t]
we have the integrable majorant
\|F^{\Lambda}_{x}(s)-F^{\infty}_{x}(s)\|_{L^{2}\oplus JL^{2}}\leq C_{t}\in L^{1}((0,t))
with
C_{t}
independent of
s
and
\Lambda
, which follows directly from
Lemma

C.9
and the fact that
\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\infty}_{0}}{\mathcal{T}}^{-1}
is bounded.

Thus as a direct consequence of (
5.16
) and (
5.18
), we get

\displaystyle\|{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}\int^{t}_{0}(\rho^{1/2}\int W-\mu_{s}^{\Lambda})ds}U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}U_{{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}}^{*}U_{t}^{\Lambda}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}tH_{\rho}}\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\Lambda}-U_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}\|\to 0\,,

(5.28)

which proves the claim for the specific initial data
\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}
and
\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\Lambda}
.

Now, let
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
and consider general initial data
\psi_{0}^{\infty}
and
\psi_{0}^{\Lambda}
with
U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}U_{0}^{\Lambda}\psi_{0}^{\Lambda}\to\psi_{0}^{\infty}
. By density, there exists a
\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}\in Q((-\Delta_{x}+x^{2}+({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{2})^{2n(1+4\alpha(1+2\epsilon))})\cap Q({\mathrm{d}}\Gamma(-\Delta))
such that

\|\psi_{0}^{\infty}-\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}\|<\tilde{\epsilon}
. As before, we set
\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\Lambda}=(U_{0}^{\Lambda})^{*}\mathbbm{1}^{\leq N}U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}\Gamma(Q_{0}^{\Lambda})\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}
.
Then from

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\Lambda}-\psi_{0}^{\Lambda}\|\leq\|\Gamma(Q_{0}^{\Lambda})\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}-U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}U_{0}^{\Lambda}\psi_{0}^{\Lambda}\|+\|\mathbbm{1}^{>N}U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}\Gamma(Q_{0}^{\Lambda})\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\Gamma(Q_{0}^{\Lambda})\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}-\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}\|+\|\psi_{0}^{\infty}-U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}U_{0}^{\Lambda}\psi_{0}^{\Lambda}\|+\|\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}-\psi_{0}^{\infty}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\|\mathbbm{1}^{>N}U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}\Gamma(Q_{0}^{\Lambda})\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}\|\,,

the strong convergence
\Gamma(Q_{0}^{\Lambda})\to 1
,
U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}U_{0}^{\Lambda}\psi_{0}^{\Lambda}\to\psi_{0}^{\infty}
, and
\mathbbm{1}^{>N}\leq{\mathcal{N}}/N
, it follows that
\|\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\Lambda}-\psi_{0}^{\Lambda}\|<2\tilde{\epsilon}
for large enough
\Lambda
. Combining this with (
5.28
), we obtain for large enough
\Lambda

\displaystyle\|{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}\int^{t}_{0}(\rho^{1/2}\int W-\mu_{s})ds}U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}U_{{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}}^{*}U_{t}^{\Lambda}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}tH_{\rho}}\psi_{0}^{\Lambda}-U_{t}^{\infty}\psi_{0}^{\infty}\|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon+\|\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\Lambda}-\psi_{0}^{\Lambda}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\|\tilde{\psi}_{0}^{\infty}-\psi_{0}^{\infty}\|\leq 4\tilde{\epsilon}\,,

which proves the claim.
∎

An explicit example of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}
satisfying
2.1
with
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}=\widecheck{{\mathcal{T}}}
is constructed in
Appendix

D
, under the additional assumption that the initial condensate
\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}
is real-valued.

In the case that
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}=\widecheck{{\mathcal{T}}}
we observe, using (
5.12
), that the infinite-volume Bogoliubov-Fröhlich Hamiltonian defined in (
1.3
) is equal to

\displaystyle H_{\infty}^{\rm BF}

\displaystyle={\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t{\mathrm{d}}\Gamma({\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\omega{\mathcal{F}})}H^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t{\mathrm{d}}\Gamma({\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\omega{\mathcal{F}})}+\left(\partial_{t}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t{\mathrm{d}}\Gamma({\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\omega{\mathcal{F}})}\right){\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t{\mathrm{d}}\Gamma({\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\omega{\mathcal{F}})}\,.

Consequently,
Theorem

2.2
follows directly from
Theorem

5.1
.

through the project SQC.

Appendix A
The Localization Function

For
0<s<1/3
and
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
we define the localization function

\Theta_{\Lambda}(x)=\frac{1}{1+|\Lambda^{-s}x|^{2n}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R})\,,

which is used to localize the condensate around the origin. It satisfies the following characterizing properties: For all
\beta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{3}
there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1

\displaystyle|\Theta_{\Lambda}|\leq C\,,\quad|\partial^{\beta}\Theta_{\Lambda}|\leq C\Lambda^{-s|\beta|}|\Theta_{\Lambda}|\,.

(A.1)

In order for
\Theta_{\Lambda}
to localize the condensate
\varphi_{0}
we have to choose
s<1/3
such that its scale is smaller than the
{\mathcal{O}}(\Lambda^{1/3})
scale of the condensate.

To facilitate our estimates, we introduce some notation from
[
14
]
, which is particularly useful when applying Young’s inequality.

Notation A.1
.

For
1\leq p_{1},\ldots,p_{M}\leq\infty
,
M\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we define the norms

\displaystyle\|f\|_{p_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge p_{M}}

\displaystyle:=\inf_{f=f_{p_{1}}+\ldots+f_{p_{M}}}\Big(\|f_{p_{1}}\|_{p_{1}}+\ldots+\|f_{p_{M}}\|_{p_{M}}\Big)\,.

and

\|f\|_{p_{1},\ldots,p_{M}}:=\|f\|_{p_{1}}+\ldots+\|f\|_{p_{M}}\,.

All relevant estimates for the localization function, obtained via Taylor expansion, are collected in the following lemma.

Lemma A.2

(Localized Estimates)
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
and
s>0
. Let
\Theta_{\Lambda}(x)=\frac{1}{1+(\Lambda^{-s}x)^{2n}}
be the localization function.

a)

(Functions flat around the origin) Let
k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
with
k\leq 2n
. For all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
, let
f:=f_{\Lambda}\in C^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},{\mathbb{C}})
. If we have flatness around the origin of
f
, namely there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
and
0\leq|\beta|\leq k-1
we have
|\partial^{\beta}f(0)|\leq\|\partial^{\beta}f\|_{\infty}C\Lambda^{-(k-|\beta|)(1/3-s)}
then

i)

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}f\|_{\infty}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\Lambda^{-k(1/3-s)}\Big(\sum_{|\beta|\leq k}\|\partial^{\beta}f\|_{\infty}\Lambda^{|\beta|/3}\Big)\,.

(A.2)

ii)

If in addition
n\geq 1
and
k\leq 2(n-1)
then there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}f\|_{2}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\Lambda^{-k(1/3-s)+3s/2}\Big(\sum_{|\beta|\leq k}\|\partial^{\beta}f\|_{\infty}\Lambda^{|\beta|/3}\Big)\,.

(A.3)

b)

(Convolution)
Then for all orders
m\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
and
f,W\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, for
1\leq p\leq\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}W\ast f\|_{2}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\sum\limits_{0\leq|\beta|\leq m-1}\|\,|y|^{|\beta|}W\|_{1,2}\|\Theta_{\Lambda}f\|_{1\wedge 2}

\displaystyle+C\Lambda^{-sm}\|\,|y|^{m}W\|_{1,2}\|f\|_{1\wedge 2}\,,

(A.4)

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}W\ast f\|_{\infty}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\sum\limits_{0\leq|\beta|\leq m-1}\|\,|y|^{|\beta|}W\|_{1,2,\infty}\|\Theta_{\Lambda}f\|_{1\wedge 2\wedge\infty}

\displaystyle+C\Lambda^{-sm}\|\,|y|^{m}W\|_{1,2,\infty}\|f\|_{1\wedge 2\wedge\infty}\,.

(A.5)

Remark A.3
.

Part b) of the lemma is required to close Grönwall-type estimates of the form

\partial_{t}\|\Theta_{\Lambda}f_{t}\|\leq\alpha(t)+\beta(t)\|\Theta_{\Lambda}f_{t}\|
, where the localization function
\Theta_{\Lambda}
has to be moved inside convolution terms of the form
W\ast f
in order to complete the argument. In this context, the terms
C\Lambda^{-sm}\|\,|y|^{m}W\|_{1,2}\|f\|_{1\wedge 2}
and
C\Lambda^{-sm}\|\,|y|^{m}W\|_{1,2,\infty}\|f\|_{1\wedge 2\wedge\infty}
appear as error terms.

Note that the flatness around the origin of the initial condensate,
2.8
, is chosen in such a way that the condensate satisfies the requirements of
Lemma

A.2
.

Proof of
Lemma

A.2
.

Part a):

The case
k=0
is trivial. Now let
k\geq 1
. By the Taylor expansion formula of
f
up to order
k-1
around 0 we have
\forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}

\exists\xi:=\xi_{x}\in[0,1]
such that

\displaystyle|(\Theta_{\Lambda}f)(x)|={}

\displaystyle\Theta_{\Lambda}(x)\bigg|\sum_{0\leq|\beta|\leq k-1}\frac{\partial^{\beta}f(0)}{\beta!}x^{\beta}+\sum_{|\beta|=k}\frac{\partial^{\beta}f(\xi x)}{\beta!}x^{\beta}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C\sum_{|\beta|\leq k}\|\partial^{\beta}f\|_{\infty}\Lambda^{-(k-|\beta|)(1/3-s)}\frac{|x|^{|\beta|}}{1+(\Lambda^{-s}|x|)^{2n}}\,,

(A.6)

where we used
|\partial^{\beta}f(0)|\leq\|\partial^{\beta}f\|_{\infty}\Lambda^{-(k-|\beta|)(1/3-s)}
,
\forall 0\leq|\beta|\leq k-1
, and the definition
\Theta_{\Lambda}(x)=\frac{1}{1+(\Lambda^{-s}|x|)^{2n}}\geq 0
.

Using
|\beta|\leq k\leq 2n
and distinguishing between the cases
|x|<\Lambda^{s}
and
|x|\geq\Lambda^{s}
we can show for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}

\displaystyle\frac{|x|^{|\beta|}}{1+(\Lambda^{-s}|x|)^{2n}}\leq\Lambda^{s|\beta|}\,.

(A.7)

The estimate (
A.2
) follows from (
A.6
) and (
A.7
).

Now we want to prove the
\|\Theta_{\Lambda}f\|_{2}
estimate. Let us assume
k\leq 2(n-1)
and thus
n\geq 1
. We use (
A.6
) and the substitution
y=\Lambda^{-s}x
to obtain

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}f\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|\Theta_{\Lambda}f(x)|^{2}dx\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-k(1/3-s)}\sum_{|\beta|\leq k}\|\partial^{\beta}f\|_{\infty}\Lambda^{|\beta|/3}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\bigg(\frac{|y|^{|\beta|}}{1+|y|^{2n}}\right)^{2}\Lambda^{3s}dy\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-k(1/3-s)+3/2s}\sum_{|\beta|\leq k}\|\partial^{\beta}f\|_{\infty}\Lambda^{|\beta|/3}\,.

Part b):

We want to change the argument of
\Theta_{\Lambda}
from
x
to
x-y
such that we can move it inside the convolution. To this end, for
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, we expand
\Theta_{\Lambda}
around the point
x-y
. By Taylor’s theorem, there exists a
\theta\in[0,1]
such that

\displaystyle|\Theta_{\Lambda}(x)|\leq{}

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{0\leq|\beta|\leq m-1}C_{m}|\Theta_{1}\left(\Lambda^{-s}(x-y)\right)|\cdot\Lambda^{-s|\beta|}\frac{|y|^{|\beta|}}{\beta!}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{|\beta|=m}C_{m}\cdot\Lambda^{-s|\beta|}\frac{|y|^{|\beta|}}{\beta!}\,,

(A.8)

where we have used (
A.1
).
Now with (
A.8
)

\displaystyle|\Theta_{\Lambda}(x)W\ast f(x)|\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{m}\sum\limits_{0\leq|\beta|\leq m-1}\int dy|y|^{|\beta|}\cdot|W(y)\Theta_{\Lambda}\left(x-y\right)f(x-y)|

\displaystyle+C_{m}\Lambda^{-sm}\int dy|y|^{m}\cdot|W(y)f(x-y)|

with Young’s inequality for
p\geq 1

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}W\ast f\|_{p}\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{m}\sum\limits_{0\leq|\beta|\leq m-1}\|\,|y|^{|\beta|}W\|_{1,p}\|\Theta_{\Lambda}f\|_{1\wedge p}

\displaystyle+C_{m}\Lambda^{-sm}\|\,|y|^{m}W\|_{1,p}\|f\|_{1\wedge p}\,.

This proves (
A.4
). The proof of (
A.5
) is analogous.
∎

Appendix B
Control of the Condensate Dynamics

To control and approximate the dynamics of the condensate
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
, we define an auxiliary function
\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}
as in
[
14
]
.

Definition B.1

(Auxiliary Function)
.

For all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
, let
\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\in H^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
. We call

\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}={\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}\left(tV\ast|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-\int_{0}^{t}\mu_{s}^{\Lambda}ds\right)}\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}

(B.1)

the auxiliary function, where
\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}
is defined in (
1.7
).

Remark B.2
.

The kinetic term in the time evolution of
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
in (
1.5
) can be omitted, as it is subleading compared to the interaction term. Specifically,

\|-\Delta\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{2}\leq C\Lambda^{1/2-2/3}\,,\quad\text{while}\quad\|V\ast|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{2}\leq C\Lambda^{1/2}\,.

Due to the appropriate scaling of
\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}
, these properties also hold for the time evolved state (see
B.7
below). This justifies the approximation of
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
through
\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}
. In particular,
\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}
and its derivatives satisfy analogous estimates as
\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}
(see e.g.
Lemma

B.5
below).

The following preliminaries (
B.2
) and (
B.3
) form the foundation of our control of the condensate.
They are adapted from bounds in
[
14
]
, and remain valid under slightly weaker conditions than considered there. Nevertheless, their proofs are analogous to those presented in
[
14
]
(see
[
41
]
for an adaptation to the setting considered here).

Proposition B.3
.

For all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
, let
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
be the solution of the Hartree equation (
1.5
). Assume that its initial data
\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}
varies on the scale
\Lambda^{1/3}
, that is,
2.6
. Then for all times
T\geq 0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
and
-T\leq t\leq T

\displaystyle\left\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{6}}\,,

(B.2)

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\,.

(B.3)

Corollary B.4
.

Assume the conditions of
B.3
. Then for all times
T\geq 0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
and
-T\leq t\leq T

\displaystyle\||\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}\|_{1\wedge 2}\leq C\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{6}}\,.

(B.4)

Proof of
B.4
.

The claim follows directly from
B.3
, the definition of the norm
\|\,.\,\|_{1\wedge 2}
(see
A.1
) and the identity
|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}=|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}+2\mathrm{Re}(\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{*}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})
, where we use
|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|=|\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}|
.
∎

B.1
Propagation Estimates

In this section, we use the auxiliary function
\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}
to approximate the condensate
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
. We begin by estimating
\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}
.

Lemma B.5

(Estimates of
\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}
)
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{3}
. For all
\Lambda\geq 1
let
\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}
be the condensate, which varies on the scale
\Lambda^{1/3}
, meaning it satisfies
2.7
|β|
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
and times
t\in{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\|\partial^{\beta}\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\Lambda^{-\frac{|\beta|}{3}}\,,

(B.5)

\displaystyle\|\partial^{\beta}\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{2}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\Lambda^{-\frac{|\beta|}{3}+\frac{1}{2}}\,.

(B.6)

Proof of
Lemma

B.5
.

The claim follows directly from the definition
\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}={\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}\left(tV\ast|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-\int_{0}^{t}\mu_{s}^{\Lambda}ds\right)}\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}
, our assumptions, and the Leibniz rule.
∎

The following
B.6
extends (
B.2
) to derivatives of
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}
, enabling us to transfer estimates from
\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}
to the condensate
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
and serves as an essential ingredient for the proof of
Lemma

B.9
in the following section.

Proposition B.6

(Approximation of
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
by
\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}
)
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{3}
. For all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
, let
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
be the solution of the Hartree equation (
1.5
). Assume that its initial data
\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}
varies on the scale
\Lambda^{1/3}
, that is,
2.6
and
2.7
|β|+2
.

Then for all times
T\geq 0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
and
-T\leq t\leq T

\displaystyle\|\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}\leq C\Lambda^{-1/6-|\beta|/3}\,.

(B.7)

Proof of
B.6
.

Let
T\geq 0
and
-T\leq t\leq T
.
We prove
B.6
by induction on
|\beta|
, denoted as
n
.

Base Case:
|\beta|=0
.

For
|\beta|=0
, the claim is the statement of
B.3
, noting that we assume
2.6
.

Induction Step:
|\beta|=n+1
.

Now let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
be fixed. Assume that the assumptions of
B.6
are fulfilled for n+1, namely
2.6
and
2.7
(n+1)+2
. Now, consider a multi-index
\beta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{3}
with
|\beta|=n+1
.

As the induction hypothesis, suppose that
B.6
holds for all multi-indices
|\tilde{\beta}|\leq n
. Thus

\|\partial^{\tilde{\beta}}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}\leq C\Lambda^{-1/6-|\tilde{\beta}|/3}\,.

We use a Grönwall estimate to get the desired bound:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\|\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Re}\Big\langle\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\,,\,\partial^{\beta}\Big((-{\mathrm{i}})\Big[-\frac{1}{2}\Delta+V\ast|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}\Big](\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\pm\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \ +{\mathrm{i}}\left(V\ast|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}\right)\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}\Big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Re}\left\langle\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\,,\,(-{\mathrm{i}})\left[\partial^{\beta}\,,\,V\ast|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}\right](\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\right\rangle

(B.8)

\displaystyle+2\mathrm{Re}\Big\langle\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\,,\,(-{\mathrm{i}})\Big[-\frac{1}{2}\Delta+V\ast|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}\Big]\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\Big\rangle

(B.9)

\displaystyle+2\mathrm{Re}\left\langle\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\,,\,(-{\mathrm{i}})\partial^{\beta}V\ast\left(|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}\right)\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}\right\rangle

(B.10)

\displaystyle+2\mathrm{Re}\Big\langle\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\,,\,(-{\mathrm{i}})\partial^{\beta}\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\Delta\Big)\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}\Big\rangle\,,

(B.11)

where we used
\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
.
The term (
B.9
) vanishes, because we only consider its real part and
\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}\in\mathbb{R}
. In the following we estimate the remaining terms (
B.8
), (
B.10
) and (
B.11
).

Given
2.7
n+3=|β|+2
, the estimate for (
B.11
) follows directly from
Lemma

B.5
.
For (
B.10
) and (
B.8
), we again use
Lemma

B.5
together with the induction hypothesis, which ensures that

\displaystyle\|\,|V|\ast\partial^{\beta}|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}\,\|_{\infty}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\Lambda^{-|\beta|/3}+C\|\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}\,,

(B.12)

\displaystyle\|\,|V|\ast\left|\partial^{\beta}(|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-|\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2})\right|\,\|_{2}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\Lambda^{-1/6-|\beta|/3}+C\|\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}\,,

(B.13)

\displaystyle\big\|-\frac{1}{2}\Delta\partial^{\beta}\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}\big\|_{2}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{|\beta|+2}{3}}\leq C\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{6}-\frac{|\beta|}{3}}\,.

(B.14)

To summarize

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\|\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}^{2}\leq{}

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:ScalarProdKommV,Phi}+\eqref{eq:ScalarProdDVConvPhi-TPhi}+\eqref{eq:ScalarProdDLaplace}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\|\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}C\left(\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{6}-\frac{|\beta|}{3}}+\|\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}\right)\,,

with Grönwall and
\tilde{\varphi}_{0}=\varphi_{0}
we get the claim
\|\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}\leq C\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{6}-\frac{\beta}{3}}
for
|\beta|=n+1
, thereby completing the induction step.
∎

Combining the estimates of
Lemma

B.5
and
B.6
we gain control of the condensate
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
as stated in the following corollary.

Corollary B.7

(Estimates of the Condensate
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
)
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{3}
. For all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
, let
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
be the solution of the Hartree equation (
1.5
). Assume that its initial data
\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}
varies on the scale
\Lambda^{1/3}
, that is,
2.6
and
2.7
|β|+2
.

Then for all times
T\geq 0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
and
-T\leq t\leq T

\displaystyle\|\partial^{\beta}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{2}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\Lambda^{1/2-|\beta|/3}\,,

(B.15)

\displaystyle\|\partial^{\beta}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{2\wedge\infty}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\Lambda^{-|\beta|/3}\,,

(B.16)

where the norm
\|\,.\,\|_{2\wedge\infty}
is defined in
A.1
.

Proof of
B.7
.

B.7
follows directly from
Lemma

B.5
and
B.6
, using
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}=\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}+(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})
.
∎

B.2
Local Stability

The goal of this section is to show that the localized condensate
\Theta_{\Lambda}|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}
can be approximated arbitrarily well by the initial condensate
\Theta_{\Lambda}|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}
, thereby improving
B.4
. Note that the localization function
\Theta_{\Lambda}
varies on the smaller scale
{\mathcal{O}}(\Lambda^{s})
, with
0<s<1/3
, localizing the condensate varying on the scale
{\mathcal{O}}(\Lambda^{1/3})
.

Proposition B.8

(Local Stability of the Condensate)
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
0<s<1/3
. Set the localization function to be
\Theta_{\Lambda}(x)=\frac{1}{1+(\Lambda^{-s}x)^{2n}}
. For all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
, let
\varphi_{t}
be the solution of the Hartree equation (
1.5
). Assume that its initial data
\varphi_{0}
varies on the scale
\Lambda^{1/3}
, that is,

2.6
,
2.7
k+3
and
2.7
i)
(k+2)+2(n-1)
. Furthermore, we require that
\varphi_{0}
is flat around the origin, namely
2.8
2(n-1),s
.

Then for all
T\geq 0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
and
-T\leq t\leq T

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}(|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2})\|_{1\wedge 2}\leq C\Big\{\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{6}-\frac{k+1}{3}-(k+1)s}+\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}s-2n(1/3-s)}\Big\}\,.

(B.17)

Proof of
B.8
.

The claim follows directly from
Lemma

B.9
below, together with the definition of the norm
\|\,.\,\|_{1\wedge 2}
(see
A.1
) and the identity
|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}=|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}+2\mathrm{Re}(\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{*}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})
with
|\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}|=|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|
.
∎

Although flatness of the initial condensate is not assumed in this section, the above approximation provides a mechanism to propagate flatness, if present in the initial data, to the evolved
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
, as for instance in
4.3
.

The estimate in
B.8
is based on the following approximation of
\Theta_{\Lambda}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
by
\Theta_{\Lambda}\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}
, which also needs control over their derivatives.

Lemma B.9
.

Assume the conditions of
B.8
. Then for all
\beta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{3}
with
|\beta|\leq k
, and
T\geq 0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
and
-T\leq t\leq T

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}\leq C\Big\{\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{6}-\frac{k+1}{3}-(k+1-|\beta|)s}+\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}s-\frac{|\beta|}{3}-2n(1/3-s)}\Big\}\,.

(B.18)

Proof of
Lemma

B.9
.

Let
T\geq 0
and
-T\leq t\leq T
.
We prove
Lemma

B.9
by induction on
k
.

Base Case:
k=0
.

For
k=0
also
\beta=0
and the proof is analogous to the argument in the induction step below. Note that the use of the induction hypothesis is not necessary as no terms
\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\gamma}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}
with
|\gamma|\leq|\beta|-1
appear.

Induction Step:
k-1
to
k
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
be fixed.
In order to estimate derivatives with greater order than
k-1
, for which we cannot use the induction hypothesis, we need an external estimate provided in
B.6
.
We use a Grönwall argument to prove the induction step. We have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Re}\Big\langle\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\,,\,\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}\Big\{(-{\mathrm{i}})\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\Delta+V\ast|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}\Big)(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\pm\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+{\mathrm{i}}\left(V\ast|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}\right)\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}\Big\}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle+2\mathrm{Re}\left\langle\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\,,\,(-{\mathrm{i}})\Theta_{\Lambda}\left[\partial^{\beta}\,,\,V\ast|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}\right](\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\right\rangle

(B.19)

\displaystyle+2\mathrm{Re}\left\langle\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\,,\,(-{\mathrm{i}})\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}V\ast\left(|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}\right)\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}\right\rangle

(B.20)

\displaystyle+2\mathrm{Re}\Big\langle\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\,,\,(-{\mathrm{i}})\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\Delta\Big)\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}\Big\rangle

(B.21)

\displaystyle-2\mathrm{Re}\left\langle\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\,,\,(-{\mathrm{i}})(\nabla\Theta_{\Lambda})\nabla\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\right\rangle\,.

(B.22)

We will now estimate
\partial_{t}\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}^{2}
by estimating the individual terms (
B.19
), (
B.20
), (
B.21
) and (
B.22
). To shorten the notation we set
\kappa_{|\beta|,k}(\Lambda):=\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{6}-\frac{k+1}{3}-(k+1-|\beta|)s}+\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}s-\frac{|\beta|}{3}-2n(1/3-s)}
.

To (
B.19
):

Consider the case
|\beta|\geq 1
. Note that (
B.19
)
β=0
=0.
We use
B.7
and the induction hypothesis
Lemma

B.9
k-1
,
|\gamma|\leq|\beta|-1\leq k-1
to conclude for all

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:ScalarProdThetaKommuDVConvPhi}_{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{\gamma=0,\,|\gamma|\leq|\beta|-1}^{\beta}C\|V\ast\partial^{\beta-\gamma}|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\gamma}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}C\kappa_{|\beta|,k}(\Lambda)\,,

(B.23)

where we used
|\gamma|s\leq(|\beta|-1)s
and
\Lambda\geq 1
.

To (
B.20
):

First note that for

we have that

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}V\ast\partial^{\beta}\left(|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}\right)\|_{2}\leq{}C\kappa_{|\beta|,k}(\Lambda)+C\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}\,,

(B.24)

which follows from the induction hypothesis

, the external estimate

,

and

and

B.5
.

We use

B.5
,
|\beta|\leq k
, and (

),

, to conclude

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}V\ast\left(|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}\right)\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma=0}^{\beta}C\Lambda^{-\frac{|\beta-\gamma|}{3}}\|\Theta_{\Lambda}V\ast\partial^{\gamma}\left(|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}\right)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma=0}^{\beta}C\Lambda^{-\frac{|\beta-\gamma|}{3}}\Big\{\kappa_{|\gamma|,k}(\Lambda)+\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\gamma}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}\Big\}

(B.25)

Then we use the induction hypothesis

k-1
for

to conclude

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:aux-eq-7}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\kappa_{|\beta|,k}(\Lambda)+C\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}\,.

Therefore for all
0\leq|\beta|\leq k

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:ScalarProdThetaDVConvPhi-Phi0}_{\beta}\leq{}

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}C\kappa_{|\beta|,k}(\Lambda)+C\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}^{2}\,.

(B.26)

To (

):

We have to estimate

. In the first step we use

which is flat around the origin to get that

(B.27)

Then it follows from

and the Leibniz rule that

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}\Delta\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{2}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\Lambda^{-2(n-1)\left(\frac{1}{3}-s\right)-\frac{|\beta|+2}{3}+\frac{3}{2}s}\,.

(B.28)

Thus
\forall 0\leq|\beta|\leq k
we have the estimate
1
1

1

This estimate is precisely the reason why we required

,

, and

.

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:ScalarProdThetaDLaplace}\leq{}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}C\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}s-\frac{|\beta|}{3}}\Lambda^{-2n\left(\frac{1}{3}-s\right)}\,.

(B.29)

To (

The desired estimates for (

build on one another: the bounds for higher derivatives propagate to the lower ones. This also explains why it is necessary to control higher derivatives in order to improve the estimate in the derivative-free case,

, which is the main objective of this lemma and used to prove

)

to get

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:ScalarProdNablaThetaNablaD}_{|\beta|=k}\leq{}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}\Lambda^{-s}\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{6}-\frac{k+1}{3}}\,,

(B.30)

where in the first step we used (

) for
\Theta_{\Lambda}
and in the second

for

.

For

the estimates obtained above imply

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}^{2}\leq{}\left(\eqref{eq:ScalarProdNablaThetaNablaD}+\eqref{eq:ScalarProdThetaDLaplace}+\eqref{eq:ScalarProdThetaDVConvPhi-Phi0}+\eqref{eq:ScalarProdThetaKommuDVConvPhi}\right)_{|\beta|=k}

\displaystyle\leq{}\left(\eqref{eq:ScalarProdNablaThetaNablaDEstimateBetaEqK}+\eqref{eq:ScalarProdThetaDLaplaceEstimate}+\eqref{eq:ScalarProdThetaDVConvPhi-Phi0Estimate}+\eqref{eq:ScalarProdThetaKommuDVConvPhiEstimate}\right)_{|\beta|=k}

\displaystyle\leq{}\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}C\kappa_{|\beta|,k}(\Lambda)+C\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\,,

with Grönwall and

we conclude for

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}\leq C\kappa_{k,k}(\Lambda)\,.

(B.31)

We have proven (
B.18
) for
|\beta|=k
. With this we have improved our estimate for
\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}
from (

)

, i.e.

B.9
k-1
, to (
B.18
)
k
. This result will now be used to improve the estimates for
0\leq\beta\leq k-1
. As we will show below, each estimate for
0\leq|\beta|\leq k-1
from our induction hypothesis
Lemma

B.9
k-1
is improved by the one corresponding to the next higher order in
|\beta|
.

Now let

and we assume that (
B.18
) holds for all
m\leq|\beta|\leq k
. In this case, we will show that (
B.18
) also holds for
|\beta|=m-1
, which proves the lemma.

Due to the estimates (
B.23
), (
B.26
) and (
B.29
), it only remains to bound (
B.22
)
|β|=m-1
.
With the help of (
B.18
) which holds for
|\beta|+1=m

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:ScalarProdNablaThetaNablaD}_{|\beta|=m-1}

\displaystyle\leq{}C\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}\Lambda^{-s}\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\nabla\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq{}\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}C\Lambda^{-s}\kappa_{|\beta|+1,k}(\Lambda)

\displaystyle\leq{}\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}C\kappa_{|\beta|,k}(\Lambda)\,.

(B.32)

With (
B.32
), (
B.29
), (
B.26
) and (

) all for

we conclude

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}C\kappa_{|\beta|,k}(\Lambda)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}^{2}\,.

From here it follows with Grönwall that for

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\partial^{\beta}(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})\|_{2}\leq C\kappa_{|\beta|,k}(\Lambda)\,.

As mentioned above, this proves the lemma.
∎

Appendix C
Supplementary Proofs

C.1
Remainder Term Estimate for Theorem
3.5

We begin with
Lemma

C.1
, which provides one of the key estimates underlying the remainder term estimate in
Lemma

C.2
, as well as the excitation number estimate in
Lemma

3.3
, and consequently
Theorem

3.5
. In particular,
Lemma

C.1
yields a result similar to
[
35
, Lemma 3.5, Part
\gamma^{c}_{N}
]
.

Lemma C.1
.

For all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
, let
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
be the condensate satisfying
\|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{2}=\Lambda^{1/2}
.
Let
\tilde{\Phi},\Phi\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},D({\mathcal{N}}))\subset L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},{\mathcal{F}}(L^{2}))
.
Then

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\tilde{\Phi},\sum_{j,k\geq 1}\Lambda{V}_{jk00}a_{j}^{*}a_{k}^{*}\Phi\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|V\|_{1}\|{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}\tilde{\Phi}\|\|{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}\Phi\|

\displaystyle\quad+\Lambda^{1/2}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\|V\|_{2}\|{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}\tilde{\Phi}\|\|\Phi\|\,.

(C.1)

Proof of
Lemma

C.1
.

For the proof we follow the ideas of
[
35
, Lemma 3.5, Part
\gamma^{c}_{N}
]
.
We compute, using the definition of
\Lambda{V}_{jk00}
and Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\tilde{\Phi},\sum_{j,k\geq 1}\Lambda{V}_{jk00}a_{j}^{*}a_{k}^{*}\Phi\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle=\Lambda\Big|\sum_{j,k\geq 1}\Big\langle\tilde{\Phi},\int dy_{1}dy_{2}V(y_{1}-y_{2})u_{0}(y_{1})u_{0}(y_{2})u_{j}^{*}(y_{1})u_{k}^{*}(y_{2})a_{j}^{*}a_{k}^{*}\Phi\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda\int dy_{1}\|\sum_{j\geq 1}u_{j}(y_{1})a_{j}\tilde{\Phi}\|\cdot|u_{0}(y_{1})|

(C.2)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\|\sum_{k\geq 1}\int dy_{2}V(y_{1}-y_{2})u_{0}(y_{2})u_{k}^{*}(y_{2})a_{k}^{*}\Phi\|\,.

(C.3)

Both terms (
C.2
) and (
C.3
) have one
u_{0}=u_{0}^{\Lambda}=\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}/\Lambda^{1/2}
term. We would like to bound both using
\|u_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\leq C\Lambda^{-1/2}
to cancel the pre-factor
\Lambda
in (
C.2
), but this is not possible for some of the terms appearing below.

We start with the estimate of (
C.3
). We want to estimate the
a_{k}^{*}
operator by
{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}
. Since this cannot be done directly for
a_{k}^{*}
, we first replace it by
a_{k}
using the CCR.
Using the notation
V_{y_{1}}(y_{2}):=V(y_{1}-y_{2})
, we find

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:Vu0u0ukakDagger-term}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|a^{*}(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}V_{y_{1}}u_{0}^{\Lambda})\Phi\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\left\langle\Phi,a^{*}(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}V_{y_{1}}u_{0}^{\Lambda})a(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}V_{y_{1}}u_{0}^{\Lambda})\Phi\right\rangle}+\|Q_{t}^{\Lambda}V_{y_{1}}u_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{2}^{2}\|\Phi\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|a(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}V_{y_{1}}u_{0}^{\Lambda})\Phi\|^{2}+\|V\|_{2}^{2}\|u_{0}^{\Lambda}\|^{2}_{\infty}\|\Phi\|^{2}\,.

(C.4)

The change in (
C.3
) from
a_{k}^{*}
to
a_{k}
is thus at the expense of the additional term in (
C.4
), coming from the CCR. We will see below that this additional term will give us the largest order in
\Lambda
.
For its estimate it is important that the integral over
y_{2}
is inside the norm in (
C.3
).
To estimate the first term in (
C.4
) together with (
C.2
) we pull
V
again outside of the annihilation operator and split all terms symmetrically. Here we can estimate both
u_{0}
in the
\|\,.\,\|_{\infty}
-Norm, since we can regularise the integrals with
|V|^{1/2}(y_{1}-y_{2})

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:Sum-ujaj-term}\cdot\|a(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}V_{y_{1}}u_{0}^{\Lambda})\Phi\|\leq\Lambda\int dy_{1}dy_{2}|V|(y_{1}-y_{2})\lvert u_{0}^{\Lambda}(y_{2})\rvert\lvert u_{0}^{\Lambda}(y_{1})\rvert

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\lVert\sum_{j\geq 1}u_{j}(y_{1})a_{j}\tilde{\Phi}\rVert\lVert\sum_{k\geq 1}u_{k}(y_{2})a_{k}\Phi\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}\bigg(\int dy_{1}dy_{2}\||V|^{1/2}(y_{1}-y_{2})\sum_{j\geq 1}u_{j}(y_{1})a_{j}\tilde{\Phi}\|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\bigg(\int dy_{1}dy_{2}\||V|^{1/2}(y_{1}-y_{2})\sum_{k\geq 1}u_{k}(y_{2})a_{k}\Phi\|\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|V\|_{1}\|{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}\tilde{\Phi}\|\|{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}\Phi\|\,,

(C.5)

in the last step we have used

\displaystyle\int dy_{2}\|\sum_{k\geq 1}u_{k}(y_{2})a_{k}\Phi\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\langle\Phi,\sum_{k\geq 1}a_{k}^{*}a_{k}\Phi\Big\rangle\leq\|{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}\Phi\|^{2}\,.

(C.6)

Now we estimate the second term in (
C.4
) in combination with (
C.2
).
Unlike above, here the potential term
\|V\|_{2}
no longer depends on
y_{1}
, and we cannot estimate
u_{0}^{\Lambda}(y_{1})
by
\|u_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}
, as we need it to use Cauchy-Schwarz in the integral over
y_{1}
:

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:Sum-ujaj-term}\cdot\|V\|_{2}\|u_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\|\Phi\|\leq\Lambda\|u_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{2}\bigg(\int dy_{1}\|\sum_{j\geq 1}u_{j}(y_{1})a_{j}\tilde{\Phi}\|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\|u_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\|V\|_{2}\|\Phi\|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{1/2}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\|V\|_{2}\|\Phi\|\|{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}\tilde{\Phi}\|\,,

(C.7)

where we have used (
C.6
).
We finally conclude

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\tilde{\Phi},\sum_{j,k\geq 1}\Lambda{V}_{jk00}a_{j}^{*}a_{k}^{*}\Phi\Big\rangle\Big|\leq\eqref{eq:Sum-ujaj-term}\cdot\eqref{eq:Vu0-L2}^{1/2}\leq\eqref{eq:qqpp-term-estimate-VL1-term-estimate}+\eqref{eq:qqpp-term-estimate-VL2-term-estimate}

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|V\|_{1}\|{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}\tilde{\Phi}\|\|{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}\Phi\|+\Lambda^{1/2}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\|V\|_{2}\|{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}\tilde{\Phi}\|\|\Phi\|\,,

which proves the claim.
∎

We proceed with the estimate of the remainder term
R_{N}
as given in (
3.11
).

Lemma C.2

(Remainder Term Estimate)
.

For all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
, let
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
be the solution of the Hartree equation (
1.5
) satisfying
2.6
. Then for all
T\geq 0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
\Lambda,\rho\geq 1
and
-T\leq t\leq T
we have

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}{\left\langle\psi^{\rm ex}_{t},R_{N}\Phi_{t}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\leq C\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\psi^{\rm ex}_{t}-\Phi_{t}\|\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{\frac{3}{2}}\Phi_{t}\|

\displaystyle\quad+C\rho^{-1}\|\psi^{\rm ex}_{t}-\Phi_{t}\|\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{2}\Phi_{t}\|\,.

(C.8)

Proof of
Lemma

C.2
.

Setting
\psi^{\rm ex}_{t}-\Phi_{t}=:\tilde{\Phi}_{t}
, we write

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}{\left\langle\psi^{\rm ex}_{t},R_{N}\Phi_{t}\right\rangle}=2\mathrm{Im}{\left\langle\psi^{\rm ex}_{t}-\Phi_{t},R_{N}\Phi_{t}\right\rangle}=2\mathrm{Im}{\left\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},R_{N}\Phi_{t}\right\rangle}\,.

We estimate this expression term by term, considering each
R_{i,N}
separately.

To
R_{4,N}
:

We estimate

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}\Big\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\rho}}{\mathcal{N}}W\ast\frac{|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}}{\Lambda}(x)\Phi_{t}\Big\rangle\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{N\Lambda}}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|W\|_{1}\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|{\mathcal{N}}\Phi_{t}\|\,.

(C.9)

Using that
\|{\mathrm{d}}\Gamma(A)\psi\|\leq\|A\|_{\rm op}\|{\mathcal{N}}\psi\|
we get for a fixed
x

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\sqrt{\rho}}\mathrm{Im}{\left\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},{\mathrm{d}}\Gamma(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}Q_{t}^{\Lambda})\Phi_{t}\right\rangle}=\frac{2}{\sqrt{\rho}}\|W\|_{\infty}\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|{\mathcal{N}}\Phi_{t}\|\,.

(C.10)

We get the
R_{4,N}
estimate from (
C.9
) and (
C.10
):

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}{\left\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},R_{4,N}\Phi_{t}\right\rangle}=2\sqrt{\frac{\Lambda}{N}}\left(\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}+1\right)(\|W\|_{1}+\|W\|_{\infty})\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|{\mathcal{N}}\Phi_{t}\|\,.

(C.11)

To
R_{3,N}
:

We insert
{\mathcal{N}}^{-1/2}{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}
to obtain

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}\bigg\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{N-{\mathcal{N}}}}{\sqrt{N}}-1\bigg)a(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda})\Phi_{t}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2\Big\|\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{N-{\mathcal{N}}}}{\sqrt{N}}-1\bigg)({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{-1/2}\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\Big\|\|a(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}){\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}\Phi_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|W\|_{2}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}N^{-1/2}\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|{\mathcal{N}}\Phi_{t}\|\,,

(C.12)

where we have used that
\|a(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda})\Phi_{t}\|\leq\sup_{x}\|Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{2}\|{\mathcal{N}}\Phi_{t}\|
and
\|(\sqrt{N-{\mathcal{N}}}-\sqrt{N})\psi\|^{2}\leq{\left\langle\psi,{\mathcal{N}}\psi\right\rangle}
.
For the Hermitian conjugate term in
R_{3,N}
we find an estimate similar to (
C.12
)
leading to

\displaystyle{\left\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},R_{3,N}\Phi_{t}\right\rangle}\leq 4N^{-1/2}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\|W\|_{2}\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)\Phi_{t}\|\,.

(C.13)

To
R_{2,N}
:

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}\Big\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},R_{2,N}\Phi_{t}\Big\rangle=2\mathrm{Im}\Big\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{mnpq\geq 1}\Lambda{V}_{mnpq}a_{m}^{*}a_{n}^{*}a_{p}a_{q}{\mathcal{N}}^{-1}{\mathcal{N}}\Phi_{t}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Lambda}{N}\bigg(\int dy_{1}dy_{2}\|\sum_{mn\geq 1}V(y_{1}-y_{2})u_{m}(y_{1})u_{n}(y_{2})a_{m}a_{n}{\mathcal{N}}^{-1}\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\bigg(\int dy_{1}dy_{2}\|\sum_{pq\geq 1}u_{p}(y_{1})u_{q}(y_{2})a_{p}a_{q}{\mathcal{N}}\Phi_{t}\|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Lambda}{N}\|V\|_{\infty}\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|{\mathcal{N}}^{2}\Phi_{t}\|\,,

(C.14)

where in the last step we have used
{\left\langle u_{p},u_{m}\right\rangle}=\delta_{p,m}
to get

\displaystyle\int dy_{1}dy_{2}\|\sum_{pq\geq 1}u_{p}(y_{1})u_{q}(y_{2})a_{p}a_{q}\psi\|^{2}=\sum_{pq\geq 1}{\left\langle\psi,a_{p}^{*}a_{q}^{*}a_{p}a_{q}\psi\right\rangle}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{p\geq 1}{\left\langle a_{q}\psi,{\mathcal{N}}a_{q}\psi\right\rangle}={\left\langle\psi,({\mathcal{N}}-1){\mathcal{N}}\psi\right\rangle}\,.

(C.15)

To
R_{1,N}
:

The remainder
R_{1,N}
consists of several terms,
see (
3.2
). In the following we will estimate each of these terms individually.

To the
\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}
terms in
R_{1,N}
:

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}{\left\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},\left[\frac{1}{2}\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}\frac{{\mathcal{N}}}{N}+\frac{1}{2}\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}\frac{{\mathcal{N}}^{2}}{N}+\mathrm{h.c.}\right]\Phi_{t}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{N}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|V\|_{1}\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|{\mathcal{N}}^{2}\Phi_{t}\|\,,

(C.16)

where we have used
{\mathbb{R}}\ni\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}=\frac{1}{2}{\left\langle\frac{\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}}{\Lambda^{1/2}},V\ast|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}\frac{\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}}{\Lambda^{1/2}}\right\rangle}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|V\|_{1}
.

To the
a(.),a^{*}(.)
Terms in
R_{1,N}
:

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}{\left\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},\left[-\frac{({\mathcal{N}}+1)\sqrt{N-{\mathcal{N}}}}{N}a\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}V\ast|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}\frac{\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}}{\Lambda^{1/2}}\right)+\mathrm{h.c.}\right]\Phi_{t}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\sqrt{N}}\|V\|_{1}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{3/2}\Phi_{t}\|\,.

(C.17)

To the
a_{j}^{*}a_{k}
Terms in
R_{1,N}
:

We get with
\|{\mathrm{d}}\Gamma(A)\psi\|\leq\|A\|_{\rm op}\|{\mathcal{N}}\psi\|
that

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}\Big\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},-\frac{1}{2}{\mathrm{d}}\Gamma(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}V\ast|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}Q_{t}^{\Lambda})\frac{{\mathcal{N}}}{N}\Phi_{t}\Big\rangle\leq\frac{\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|^{2}_{\infty}}{N}\|V\|_{1}\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|{\mathcal{N}}^{2}\Phi_{t}\|\,.

(C.18)

The
K_{1}(t)
term in
R_{1,N}
is estimated in the following

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}\Big\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},-\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\mathcal{N}}}{N}{\mathrm{d}}\Gamma(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}K_{1}^{\Lambda}(t)Q_{t}^{\Lambda})\Phi_{t}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{Im}\Big\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{mn\geq 1}\Lambda{V}_{0mn0}a_{m}^{*}a_{n}\frac{{\mathcal{N}}}{N}\Phi_{t}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle\times\int dy_{1}dy_{2}\Big|\sum_{m,n\geq 1}\Big\langle|V|^{\frac{1}{2}}(y_{1}-y_{2})u_{m}(y_{1})a_{m}\tilde{\Phi}_{t},|V|^{\frac{1}{2}}(y_{1}-y_{2})u_{n}(y_{2})a_{n}{\mathcal{N}}\Phi_{t}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|^{2}_{\infty}}{N}\|V\|_{1}\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{2}\Phi_{t}\|\,.

(C.19)

Putting both estimates (
C.18
) and (
C.19
) together we conclude

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}\bigg\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},\Big[-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{mn\geq 1}\Lambda{V}_{m0n0}a_{m}^{*}a_{n}\frac{{\mathcal{N}}}{N}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{mn\geq 1}\Lambda{V}_{0mn0}a_{m}^{*}a_{n}\frac{{\mathcal{N}}}{N}+\mathrm{h.c.}\Big]\Phi_{t}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 4\frac{\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{N}\|V\|_{1}\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{2}\Phi_{t}\|\,,

(C.20)

where we used that the h.c. terms coincide with the original terms due to
V(y_{1}-y_{2})=V(y_{2}-y_{1})
.

To the
a_{m}^{*}a_{n}^{*},a_{m}a_{n}
terms in
R_{1,N}
:

The estimates given here rely heavily on
Lemma

C.1
. By using
Lemma

C.1
,
