0\leq-\sqrt{(N-{\mathcal{N}})(N-{\mathcal{N}}-1)}+N\leq-(N-{\mathcal{N}}-1)+N={\mathcal{N}}+1
and inserting
({\mathcal{N}}+2)^{-1/2}({\mathcal{N}}+2)^{1/2}
before
\Phi_{t}
we get

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}\bigg\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},\frac{1}{2}\sum_{mn\geq 1}\Lambda{V}_{mn00}a_{m}^{*}a_{n}^{*}\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{(N-{\mathcal{N}})(N-{\mathcal{N}}-1)}}{N}-1\bigg)\Phi_{t}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}C\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|V\|_{1}\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{2}\Phi_{t}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\Lambda^{1/2}}{N}C\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\|V\|_{2}\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{3/2}\Phi_{t}\|\,.

(C.21)

The conjugate term can be estimated with the same argument as above with
\tilde{\Phi}_{t}
and
\Phi_{t}
interchanged and inserting
({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{-3/2}({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{3/2}
before
\Phi_{t}
:

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}\bigg\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{(N-{\mathcal{N}})(N-{\mathcal{N}}-1)}}{N}-1\bigg)\frac{1}{2}\sum_{mn\geq 1}\Lambda{V}_{00mn}a_{m}a_{n}\Phi_{t}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|V\|_{1}\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{2}\Phi_{t}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\Lambda^{1/2}}{N}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\|V\|_{2}\|({\mathcal{N}}+3)^{-1/2}\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{2}\Phi_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\Lambda^{1/2}}{N}(\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}+\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2})(\|V\|_{1}+\|V\|_{2})\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{2}\Phi_{t}\|\,.

(C.22)

Note that (
C.22
) has larger prefactor than (
C.21
), namely
\Lambda^{1/2}/N
instead of
1/N
for the
({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{2}
term. However, this is not important, as
R_{2,N}
already introduces a larger prefactor
\Lambda/N
for a
({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{2}
term, due to (
C.14
).

To the
a_{m}^{*}a_{n}a_{p},a_{m}^{*}a_{n}^{*}a_{p}
terms in
R_{1,N}
:

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}\bigg\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},\sum_{mnp\geq 1}\Lambda{V}_{0mnp}\frac{\sqrt{N-{\mathcal{N}}}}{N}a_{m}^{*}a_{n}a_{p}\Phi_{t}\bigg\rangle\leq 2\Lambda^{1/2}\|\varphi_{t}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\int dy_{1}dy_{2}\|\sum_{m\geq 1}V(y_{1}-y_{2})u_{m}(y_{2})a_{m}\frac{\sqrt{N-{\mathcal{N}}}}{N}\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

(C.23)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\int dy_{1}dy_{2}\|\sum_{np\geq 1}u_{n}(y_{1})u_{p}(y_{2})a_{n}a_{p}\Phi_{t}\|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\,.

(C.24)

Now we estimate both terms (
C.23
) and (
C.24
) separately.

\displaystyle\eqref{R1N-a-STAR-a-a-1-term}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int dy_{1}dy_{2}\sum_{mn\geq 1}V^{2}(y_{1}-y_{2})u_{n}^{*}(y_{2})u_{m}(y_{2})\Big\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},a_{n}^{*}a_{m}\frac{N-{\mathcal{N}}}{N^{2}}\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\|V\|_{2}^{2}\sum_{m\geq 1}\Big\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},a_{m}^{*}a_{m}\frac{N-{\mathcal{N}}}{N^{2}}\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\Big\rangle\leq\|V\|_{2}^{2}\Big\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},\frac{{\mathcal{N}}}{N}\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\Big\rangle\,,

(C.25)

where we have used that
(N-{\mathcal{N}})/N\leq 1
. Now we estimate the second term

\displaystyle\eqref{R1N-a-STAR-a-a-2-term}^{2}={}

\displaystyle\int dy_{1}dy_{2}\sum_{mpnq\geq 1}u_{n}(y_{1})u_{p}(y_{2})u_{m}^{*}(y_{1})u_{q}^{*}(y_{2}){\left\langle\Phi_{t},a_{m}^{*}a_{q}^{*}a_{n}a_{p}\Phi_{t}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{np\geq 1}{\left\langle\Phi_{t},a_{n}^{*}a_{p}^{*}a_{n}a_{p}\Phi_{t}\right\rangle}\leq{\left\langle\Phi_{t},{\mathcal{N}}^{2}\Phi_{t}\right\rangle}\,.

(C.26)

We conclude

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}\bigg\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},\sum_{mnp\geq 1}\Lambda{V}_{0mnp}\frac{\sqrt{N-{\mathcal{N}}}}{N}a_{m}^{*}a_{n}a_{p}\Phi_{t}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2\Lambda^{1/2}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\eqref{R1N-a-STAR-a-a-1-term-estimate}^{1/2}\cdot\eqref{R1N-a-STAR-a-a-2-term-estimate}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\Lambda^{1/2}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\frac{1}{N^{1/2}}\|V\|_{2}\|{\mathcal{N}}^{1/2}\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|{\mathcal{N}}\Phi_{t}\|\,.

(C.27)

Analogously one finds the same estimate for the conjugate term.
Now by inserting
({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{-1/2}({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{1/2}
before
\Phi_{t}
at the start of our estimate in (
C.27
) and
({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{-1}({\mathcal{N}}+1)
for the conjugate term, we conclude

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}\bigg\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},\Big(\sum_{mnp\geq 1}\Lambda{V}_{0mnp}\frac{\sqrt{N-{\mathcal{N}}}}{N}a_{m}^{*}a_{n}a_{p}+\mathrm{h.c.}\Big)\Phi_{t}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{\Lambda^{1/2}}{N^{1/2}}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\|V\|_{2}\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{3/2}\Phi_{t}\|\,.

(C.28)

R_{1,N}
estimate conclusion:

We now collect all estimates for the terms in
R_{1,N}
, i.e. (
C.16
), (
C.17
), (
C.20
), (
C.21
), (
C.22
), (
C.28
) and
group contributions by the order of
{\mathcal{N}}+1
. In doing so, we use the bounds
1/N\leq\Lambda^{1/2}/N\leq\Lambda^{1/2}/N^{1/2}
, valid for
\Lambda,N\geq 1
. This leads to

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Im}{\left\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{t},R_{1,N}\Phi_{t}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{\Lambda}{N}\right)^{1/2}\left(\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}+\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}\right)\left(\|V\|_{1}+\|V\|_{2}\right)\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{3/2}\Phi_{t}\|

\displaystyle+C\frac{\Lambda^{1/2}}{N}\left(\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}+\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}\right)\left(\|V\|_{1}+\|V\|_{2}\right)\|\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\|\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{2}\Phi_{t}\|\,,

(C.29)

which concludes our estimate for
R_{1,N}
.

Collecting the different
R_{i,N}
estimates done above, i.e. (
C.11
), (
C.13
), (
C.14
), (
C.29
) and using
\Lambda\geq 1
to simplify, yields the
R_{N}
estimate after applying
\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\leq C
(see
B.3
).
∎

C.2
Proof of Lemma
3.3

The proof of
Lemma

3.3
, estimating the excitation number, is given below.

Proof of
Lemma

3.3
.

We use a Grönwall estimate. To shorten the notation we write
\psi_{\Lambda,t}^{\mathrm{BF}}=:\psi_{t}
. We consider

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}\ni\partial_{t}{\left\langle\psi_{t}\,,\,({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{n}\psi_{t}\right\rangle}=\mathrm{Re}{\left\langle\psi_{t}\,,\,-{\mathrm{i}}\left[({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{n},H^{\rm BF}_{\Lambda}\right]\psi_{t}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Im}\Big\langle\psi_{t}\,,\,\Big[({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{n},\frac{1}{2}\sum_{mn}\left((K_{2}^{\Lambda}(t)J)_{mn}a_{m}^{*}a_{n}^{*}+{\rm h.c.}\right)\Big]\psi_{t}\Big\rangle

(C.30)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathrm{Im}{\left\langle\psi_{t}\,,\,\left[({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{n},a^{*}(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda})+a(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda})\right]\psi_{t}\right\rangle}\,.

(C.31)

We now estimate both terms (
C.30
) and (
C.31
) separately.
For (
C.31
) we use
({\mathcal{N}}+b)^{m}-a^{m}\leq(1+b)^{m}{\mathcal{N}}^{m-1}
,
\forall m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
b\geq 0
, to obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:N-single-a-a*-Term}=2\mathrm{Im}{\left\langle\psi_{t}\,,\,a^{*}(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda})\{({\mathcal{N}}+2)^{n}-({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{n}\}\psi_{t}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{Im}{\left\langle\{({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{n}-{\mathcal{N}}^{n}\}^{1/2}\psi_{t}\,,\,a^{*}(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda})\{({\mathcal{N}}+2)^{n}-({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{n}\}^{1/2}\psi_{t}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\leq C\|W\|_{2}\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\psi_{t}\|^{2}+C\|W\|_{2}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{\frac{n}{2}}\psi_{t}\|^{2}\,.

(C.32)

The estimate of (
C.30
) follows from
Lemma

C.1
and the identity

\sum_{mn\geq 0}(K_{2}^{\Lambda}(t)J)_{mn}a_{m}^{*}a_{n}^{*}=\sum_{mn\geq 1}\Lambda{V}_{mn00}a_{m}^{*}a_{n}^{*},
yielding

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:N-a*a*-Term}\leq{}

\displaystyle C(\|V\|_{1}+\|V\|_{2})\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{\frac{n}{2}}\psi_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+C\Lambda\|V\|_{2}\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\psi_{t}\|^{2}\,.

(C.33)

This finally leads us to

\displaystyle\partial_{t}{\left\langle\psi_{t}\,,\,({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{n}\psi_{t}\right\rangle}\leq\eqref{eq:N-a*a*-Term}+\eqref{eq:N-single-a-a*-Term}\leq\eqref{eq:N-single-a-a*-Term-estimate}+\eqref{eq:N-a*a*-Term-estimate}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}^{2}(\|V\|_{1}+\|V\|_{2}+\|W\|_{2})\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{\frac{n}{2}}\psi_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C(\Lambda\|V\|_{2}+\|W\|_{2})\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\psi_{t}\|^{2}\,.

(C.34)

From (
C.34
) and (
B.3
) we are now able to prove by induction on
n
, using Grönwall’s inequality, that

\displaystyle{\left\langle\psi_{t}\,,\,({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{n}\psi_{t}\right\rangle}\leq C{\left\langle\psi_{0}\,,\,\big(\Lambda+({\mathcal{N}}+1)\big)^{n}\psi_{0}\right\rangle}\,,\quad\forall n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\,.

(C.35)

∎

C.3
Proof of Lemma
4.5

Proof of
Lemma

4.5
.

Let
U_{\Lambda,t}^{\rm Bog}=U_{{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}}
be the propagator of the Bogoliubov dynamics. We write

{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}=:\begin{pmatrix}c^{\Lambda}&J^{*}b^{\Lambda}J^{*}\\
b^{\Lambda}&Jc^{\Lambda}J^{*}\end{pmatrix}\,,\quad{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}=:\begin{pmatrix}U_{t}^{\Lambda}&J^{*}V_{t}^{\Lambda}J^{*}\\
V_{t}^{\Lambda}&JU_{t}^{\Lambda}J^{*}\end{pmatrix}\,.

Set
f^{\Lambda}:=\big(t\mapsto(x\mapsto(c^{\Lambda}+J^{*}b^{\Lambda})((U_{t}^{\Lambda})^{*}-(V_{t}^{\Lambda})^{*}J)Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda})\big)
and note that
({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}=S({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{*}S
.
In
Lemma

C.9
we prove the following regularity and bounds for
f^{\Lambda}
:

a)

For almost all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have
(t\mapsto f_{t}^{\Lambda}(x,\,.\,))\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{t},L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{y}))
.

b)

For all times
T\geq 0
there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
,
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
and
-T\leq t\leq T

\displaystyle\sum_{|\beta|\leq M}(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x}f_{t,x}^{\Lambda}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}+(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\|\partial_{t}f_{t,x}^{\Lambda}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq C\,.

(C.36)

The bound on the Hamiltonian by
\pm h_{\rm oc}
in (
4.16
) follows directly from
0\leq-\Delta_{x}\leq h_{\rm oc}
,
\pm A(f_{t,x}^{\Lambda}\oplus Jf_{t,x}^{\Lambda})\leq 2(\sup_{x}(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\|f_{t,x}^{\Lambda}\|_{2})(1+x^{2})^{1/4}({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{1/2}
,
(1+x^{2})^{1/4}({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{1/2}\leq h_{\rm oc}
and the estimate (
C.36
) on
f
.

The regularity of
\big(t\mapsto\big\langle\psi,\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda.{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}}(t)\psi\big\rangle\big)\in C^{1}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})
follows directly from the regularity of
f^{\Lambda}
. The bound in (
4.18
) can immediately be seen by calculating the derivative
\frac{d}{dt}\big\langle\psi,\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda.{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}}(t)\psi\big\rangle
explicitly.

Thus, it remains to verify the commutator estimate for
\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}(t)
and
h_{\rm oc}^{M}
as in (
4.17
). To this end, let
\psi\in\bigcup_{L\geq 0}\bigoplus_{n=0}^{L}{\mathscr{S}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{C}})\bar{\otimes}L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\bar{\otimes}_{s}n}=:D
and
f\in C_{b}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d},L^{2})
. We prove that there exists a constant
C_{M}
only dependent on
M
such that

\displaystyle\left|{\left\langle\psi,[-\Delta_{x},h_{\rm oc}^{M}]\psi\right\rangle}\right|\leq C_{M}{\left\langle\psi,h_{\rm oc}^{M}\psi\right\rangle}\,,

(C.37)

\displaystyle\left|{\left\langle A(f_{t,x}\oplus Jf_{t,x})\psi,h_{\rm oc}^{M}\psi\right\rangle}-{\left\langle h_{\rm oc}^{M}\psi,A(f_{t,x}\oplus Jf_{t,x})\psi\right\rangle}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{M}\Big(\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\sum_{|\beta|\leq M}(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x}f_{t,x}\|_{2}\Big){\left\langle\psi,h_{\rm oc}^{M}\psi\right\rangle}\,.

(C.38)

These estimates can be extended to all
\psi\in D(h_{\rm oc}^{M})
and all
f
satisfying
\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\sum_{|\beta|\leq M}(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x}f_{t,x}\|_{2}<\infty
, and hence, in particular, to
f^{\Lambda}
, by a standard density argument. This will prove (
4.17
) and therefore
Lemma

4.5
.
It remains to prove (
C.37
) and (
C.38
).

We start with the proof of (
C.38
).
We begin with the case of
M=:2m
even. This allows us to symmetrically split
h_{\rm oc}^{2m}=:h^{2m}
between the arguments of the scalar product. Using the recursive definition of the iterated commutator, namely
\mathrm{ad}_{h}^{(0)}(a^{\#}(f_{x})):=a^{\#}(f_{x})
and
\mathrm{ad}_{h}^{(k)}(a^{\#}(f_{x}))\psi:=\big[\mathrm{ad}_{h}^{(k-1)}(a^{\#}(f_{x})),h\big]\psi
for
k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
,
we commute the
h
operators with
a^{\#}(f_{x})
step by step, applying this definition
m
-times. This yields an expansion of the commutator terms in (
C.38
):

\displaystyle\Big|{\left\langle(a^{\#}(f_{x}))^{*}\psi,h^{2m}\psi\right\rangle}-{\left\langle h^{2m}\psi,a^{\#}(f_{x})\psi\right\rangle}\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}{m\choose k}\Big\{\big\langle h^{k}\psi,\mathrm{ad}_{h}^{(m-k)}(a^{\#}(f_{x}))h^{m}\psi\big\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\big\langle h^{m}\psi,(-1)^{m-k}\mathrm{ad}_{h}^{(m-k)}(a^{\#}(f_{x}))h^{k}\psi\big\rangle\Big\}\Big|\,,

(C.39)

where the
k=m
terms cancel, as they have identical numbers of
h
operators on both sides of the scalar product.
To proceed, we need the following representation for the iterated commutator (for
m-k\geq 1
):

\displaystyle\mathrm{ad}_{h}^{(m-k)}(a^{\#}(f_{x}))\psi=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta|+|\gamma|+|\delta|+l\leq 2(m-k)\\
|\gamma|+|\delta|+l\leq 2(m-k)-1\end{subarray}}C_{\beta,\gamma,\delta,l}^{(m-k)}a^{\#}(D_{x}^{\beta}f_{x}){\mathcal{N}}^{l}x^{\gamma}D_{x}^{\delta}\psi\,.

(C.40)

This representation is not valid for
m-k=0
, as
\mathrm{ad}_{h}^{(m-k)}(a^{\#}(f_{x}))=a^{\#}(f_{x})
. Thus, it is essential that the sum in (
C.39
) only runs over
k\leq m-1
.

A key feature of (
C.40
) is that the combined order of
{\mathcal{N}}^{l}x^{\gamma}D_{x}^{\delta}
is strictly less than the order of monomials in
h^{m-k}
, allowing us to ultimately close the estimate.
(
C.40
) can be verified by induction and readily checked for
m-k=1
using the definition of the harmonic oscillator
h
.

We now combine the representation (
C.40
) with the inequality

\displaystyle(-iD_{x})^{\delta}x^{\gamma}{\mathcal{N}}^{2l}x^{\gamma}(-iD_{x})^{\delta}\leq Ch^{|\delta|+|\gamma|+l}\,,

(C.41)

which follows from the definition of the harmonic oscillator
h=-\Delta_{x}+x^{2}+({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{2}
by an induction argument on
(|\gamma|,|\delta|,l)
.
Applying this, we estimate (
C.39
):

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{ad}_{h}^{(m-k)}(a^{\#}(f_{x}))h^{k}\psi\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta|+|\gamma|+|\delta|+l\leq 2(m-k)\\
|\gamma|+|\delta|+l\leq 2(m-k)-1\end{subarray}}C_{m,k}\big(\sup_{x}(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\|D_{x}^{\beta}f_{x}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\times\|(1+x^{2})^{1/4}({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{1/2+l}x^{\gamma}D_{x}^{\delta}h^{k}\psi\|

\displaystyle\overset{\clap{\eqref{eq:aux-eq-67}}}{\leq}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta|+|\gamma|+|\delta|+l\leq 2(m-k)\\
|\gamma|+|\delta|+l\leq 2(m-k)-1\end{subarray}}C_{m,k}\big(\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x}f_{t,x}\|_{2}\big)C\|h^{\frac{|\delta|+|\gamma|+l+1}{2}}h^{k}\psi\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,k}\Big(\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\sum_{|\beta|\leq M}(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x}f_{t,x}\|_{2}\Big)\|h^{m}\psi\|\,.

(C.42)

We conclude from (
C.39
) and (
C.42
)

\displaystyle\big|{\left\langle(a^{\#}(f_{x}))^{*}\psi,h^{2m}\psi\right\rangle}-{\left\langle h^{2m}\psi,a^{\#}(f_{x})\psi\right\rangle}\big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m}\Big(\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\sum_{|\beta|\leq M}(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x}f_{t,x}\|_{2}\Big)\|h^{m}\psi\|^{2}\,.

This proves the bound (
C.38
) in the case of even
M=2m
for all
f\in C_{b}^{\infty}
.

Now we consider the case
M=2m+1
odd.
To avoid commuting
a^{\#}(f_{x})
with
h^{1/2}
, whose commutator is not directly accessible, we rewrite
h
in the following form:

\displaystyle h=(-\nabla_{x}+x)(\nabla_{x}+x)+({\mathcal{N}}+1)({\mathcal{N}}+1)+3\,.

(C.43)

To keep the argument simple, we restrict ourselves to the case of
m=0
.
We use the decomposition (
C.43
) to get similarly to (
C.39
) that

\displaystyle\Big|{\left\langle(a^{\#}(f_{x}))^{*}\psi,h\psi\right\rangle}-{\left\langle h\psi,a^{\#}(f_{x})\psi\right\rangle}\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\sum_{B\in\{(\nabla_{x}+x),({\mathcal{N}}+1)\}}\Big(\mathrm{Im}{\left\langle\psi,\Big[a^{\#}(f_{x}),B^{*}\big]B\psi\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathrm{Im}{\left\langle B\psi,\big[a^{\#}(f_{x}),B^{*}\big]\psi\right\rangle}\Big)\Big|\,.

(C.44)

The commutators in (
C.44
) can be estimated directly, proving (
C.38
) for
M=1
.
The result then extends to all odd
M=2m+1
by analogy with the even case.

With the use of the following identity,

\displaystyle\mathrm{ad}_{h}^{(k)}(-\Delta_{x})\psi

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\gamma|+|\delta|\leq 2}C_{\gamma,\delta}^{(k)}x^{\gamma}D_{x}^{\delta}\psi\,,\quad\forall k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}\,,

(C.45)

instead of the representation of the iterated commutator in (
C.40
) the proof of the estimate (
C.37
) follows in a similar fashion to the proof of the (
C.38
) estimate shown above.
∎

C.4
Proofs of the Infinite-Volume Approximation

The following lemma establishes the convergence, as
\Lambda\to\infty
, of the terms that appear in
\widetilde{H}^{\mathrm{BF}}_{\Lambda,{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}}(t)
and in the generator of
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}
.

Lemma C.3
.

For all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
, let
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
be the solution of the Hartree equation (
1.5
). Assume that its initial data
\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}
varies on the scale
\Lambda^{1/3}
, that is,

2.6
,
2.7
i)
2
. Furthermore, we require that
\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}
is flat around the origin, namely
2.8
2,s
,
0<s<1/3
, and assume that
\exists\eta\in H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
,
\delta,C>0
such that
\forall\Lambda\geq 1

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}(\Lambda^{1/3}\,.\,)-\eta\|_{2}\leq C\Lambda^{-\delta}\,.

(C.46)

We set

\displaystyle\gamma=\min\!\left\{\delta,\,s,\,\tfrac{3}{2}\bigl(\tfrac{1}{3}-s\bigr),\,\tfrac{1}{6}\right\}\,.

(C.47)

Then we have for all
T\geq 0
that there exists
C>0
such that for all
\Lambda\geq 1
and
f\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
with
y^{2}f\in L^{2}

\displaystyle\|(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}-1)f\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-3/2(1/3-s)}\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}\,,

(C.48)

\displaystyle|\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}-\mu^{\infty}|

\displaystyle\leq C(\Lambda^{-\delta}+\Lambda^{-1/6})\,,

(C.49)

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)}\right)f\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma}\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\Lambda^{-1/6}\|f\|_{\infty}\,,\quad\text{if }f\in L^{\infty}

(C.50)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\left(K_{1}^{\Lambda}(t)-K_{1}^{\infty}\right)f\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma}\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}

(C.51)

\displaystyle\left\|\left(K_{2}^{\Lambda}(t)-K_{2}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-2{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)}\right)Jf\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma}\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}\,.

(C.52)

If in addition
2.7
4
is satisfied then

\displaystyle\Big\|\Big(\left({\mathrm{i}}\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\right)-\Big(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\Big){\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)}\Big)f\Big\|_{2}\leq C\Lambda^{-1/6}\|f\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+C\Lambda^{-\gamma}\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}\,,\quad\text{if }f\in L^{\infty},

(C.53)

and

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathrm{i}}\partial_{t}Q_{t}^{\Lambda}f\right\|_{2}\leq C\left(\Lambda^{-1/6}+\Lambda^{-1/2(1-3s)}\right)\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}\,.

(C.54)

Proof.

To prove the lemma, we localize the condensate around the origin, where it is approximately flat:
|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|\sim 1
(see
B.4
and
2.8
). For this purpose, we use the localization function with
n=1
and
0<s<1/3
:
\Theta_{\Lambda}(y)=1/(1+(\Lambda^{-s}y)^{2})
as defined in
Appendix

A
.

Let
T\geq 0
and
-T\leq t\leq T
.
We begin with the estimate of (
C.48
)

\displaystyle\|(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}-1)f\|_{2}=\frac{1}{\Lambda}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{2}|{\left\langle\Theta_{\Lambda}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda},\Theta_{\Lambda}^{-1}f\right\rangle}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}}{\Lambda^{1/2}}\|\Theta_{\Lambda}\|_{2}\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}\leq C\Lambda^{-3/2(1/3-s)}\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}\,.

Next, we prove
|\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}-\mu^{\infty}|\leq C(\Lambda^{-\delta}+\Lambda^{-1/6})
. We have

\displaystyle\mu_{0}^{\Lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\Lambda}\int|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}(x)V(y)|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}(x-y)dxdy

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}{\left\langle|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}(\Lambda^{1/3}\,.\,),\Lambda V(\Lambda^{1/3}\,.\,)\ast|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}(\Lambda^{1/3}\,.\,)\right\rangle}\,,

after the rescaling
x\to\Lambda^{1/3}x
and
y\to\Lambda^{1/3}y
.
Using assumption (
C.46
), we get

\displaystyle\Big|\mu_{0}^{\Lambda}-\frac{1}{2}{\left\langle|\eta|^{2},\Lambda V(\Lambda^{1/3}\,.\,)\ast|\eta|^{2}\right\rangle}\Big|\leq\|V\|_{2,\infty}\Lambda^{-\delta}\,.

(C.55)

The convolution with
\Lambda V(\Lambda^{1/3}\,.\,)
acts as an approximation of the identity, yielding

\displaystyle\Big|{\left\langle|\eta|^{2},\Lambda V(\Lambda^{1/3}\,.\,)\ast|\eta|^{2}\right\rangle}-\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle|\eta|^{2},|\eta|^{2}\int V\Big\rangle\Big|\leq C\Lambda^{-1/3}\||y|V\|_{1}\|\eta\|_{H^{1}}^{4}\,.

(C.56)

By
B.4
, we also have
|\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}-\mu_{0}^{\Lambda}|\leq C\|V\|_{1,2}\Lambda^{-1/6}
, which shows (
C.49
).

We now prove (
C.50
). For this we first estimate
\|V\ast(|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-1)f\|_{2}\leq\|\Theta_{\Lambda}V\ast(|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-1)\|_{\infty}\|1/\Theta_{\Lambda}f\|_{2}
, and following the proof of
4.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|V\ast(|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-1)f\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\Lambda^{-s}+\Lambda^{-2(1/3-s)}+\Lambda^{-1/6}\big)\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}\,.

(C.57)

We proceed by showing the claim for
\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}
and subsequently extend it to
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
. Using
Lemma

A.2
a), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}-{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}\left(tV\ast|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-\int_{0}^{t}\mu_{s}^{\Lambda}ds\right)}\right)f\right\|_{2}=\|(\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}-1)f\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Theta_{\Lambda}(\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}-1)\|_{\infty}\|1/\Theta_{\Lambda}f\|_{2}\leq C\Lambda^{-2(1/3-s)}\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}\,.

(C.58)

Next, using
|{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}x}-1|\leq|x|
, (
C.49
) and (
C.57
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\left({\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}\left(tV\ast|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-\int_{0}^{t}\mu_{s}^{\Lambda}ds\right)}-{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)}\right)f\right\|_{2}\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma}\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}\,.

(C.59)

To estimate
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
by the simplified phase, we insert
\pm\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}
and apply (
C.58
), (
C.59
), and
B.3
, yielding

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)}\right)f\right\|_{2}\leq\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{2}\|f\|_{\infty}+\|(\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}-1)f\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\left({\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}\left(tV\ast|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-\int_{0}^{t}\mu_{s}^{\Lambda}ds\right)}-{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)}\right)f\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-1/6}\|f\|_{\infty}+C\big(\Lambda^{-2(1/3-s)}+\Lambda^{-\gamma}\big)\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}\,,

which proves (
C.50
). In the same way, estimating
\|(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda})f\|_{1\wedge 2}\leq\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-\tilde{\varphi}_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{2}\|f\|_{2}
, yields

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)}\right)f\right\|_{1\wedge 2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma}\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}\,.

(C.60)

This estimate is used to prove (
C.51
) and (
C.52
).

Now, we prove (
C.51
). Using (
C.48
),
K_{1}^{\Lambda}(t)=Q_{t}^{\Lambda}\tilde{K}_{1}^{\Lambda}(t)Q_{t}^{\Lambda}
, as well as
\|\tilde{K}_{1}^{\Lambda}(t)\|_{\rm op}\leq C
, we have

\displaystyle\|(K_{1}^{\Lambda}(t)-K_{1}^{\infty})f\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|Q_{t}^{\Lambda}\tilde{K}_{1}^{\Lambda}\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}-1\right)f\|_{2}+\|Q_{t}^{\Lambda}\big(\tilde{K}_{1}^{\Lambda}-K_{1}^{\infty}\big)f\|_{2}+\|\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}-1\right)K_{1}^{\infty}f\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-1/2(1-3s)}\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}+\|(\tilde{K}_{1}^{\Lambda}-K_{1}^{\infty})f\|_{2}\,.

(C.61)

The last term is estimated using (
C.50
) and (
C.60
)

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{K}_{1}^{\Lambda}-K_{1}^{\infty})f\|_{2}=\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}V\ast((\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda})^{*}f)-V\ast f\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}V\ast\left({\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)}f\right)-V\ast f\right\|_{2}+\left\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}V\ast\left((\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda})^{*}f-{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)}f\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma}\big(\|(1+y^{2})V\ast f\|_{2}+\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}\big)+C\Lambda^{-1/6}\|V\ast f\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma}\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}\,,

(C.62)

where we used
\|(1+y^{2})V\ast f\|_{2}=C\|(1-\Delta)\widehat{V}\widehat{f}\|_{2}\leq C\|(1+y^{2})V\|_{2}\|(1+y^{2})f\|_{2}
.
We conclude
\|(K_{1}^{\Lambda}(t)-K_{1}^{\infty})f\|_{2}\leq\eqref{eq:aux-eq-113}
. Analogously, one shows (
C.52
).

From here on, we additionally assume
2.7
4
. We start by proving (
C.53
). This estimate follows from the Hartree equation,
{\mathrm{i}}\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}=h_{t}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
, where
h_{t}=-\frac{\Delta}{2}+V\ast|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}|^{2}-\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}
, together with the preceding estimates and
B.7
|β|=2
.

Finally, the bound of (
C.54
) is obtained analogously, using
{\mathrm{i}}\partial_{t}Q_{t}^{\Lambda}f=\Lambda^{-1}h_{t}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}{\left\langle\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda},f\right\rangle}-\Lambda^{-1}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}{\left\langle-h_{t}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda},f\right\rangle}
and applying the same type of estimates as in (
C.53
).
∎

By a density argument, together with the uniform boundedness of the operators appearing in
Lemma

C.3
, the convergence extends immediately to the whole
L^{2}
-space.

Corollary C.4
.

Under the conditions of
Lemma

C.3
we have for
f\in L^{2}
that, as
\Lambda\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\|(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}-1)f\|_{2}+|\mu_{t}^{\Lambda}-\mu^{\infty}|+\left\|\left(\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}-{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)}\right)f\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\to 0\,,

(C.63)

\displaystyle\|(K_{1}^{\Lambda}(t)-K_{1}^{\infty})f\|_{2}+\|(K_{2}^{\Lambda}(t)-K_{2}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-2{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)})Jf\|_{2}

\displaystyle\to 0\,.

(C.64)

Consequently, in the infinite-volume limit, all functions in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\setminus\{0\}
are not in the condensate and thus represent excitations. This is because the condensate becomes a constant phase (see (
C.63
)) and therefore no longer belongs to
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
. This can also be seen from the strong convergence of the projection
Q_{t}^{\Lambda}
to the identity.

To prove the convergence of
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}
to
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}
we need the following lemma.

Lemma C.5
.

Let
{\mathcal{V}}^{\infty}_{t}
be from (
5.6
).
Set
h:=-\Delta+y^{2}+1
then
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}\big(D(h)\oplus JD(h)\big)=D(h)\oplus JD(h)
, and for all
T\geq 0
there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
F\in D(h)\oplus JD(h)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[-T,T]}\big\|\big(h\oplus JhJ^{*}\big){\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\big\|_{L^{2}\oplus JL^{2}}\leq C\big\|\big(h\oplus JhJ^{*}\big)F\big\|_{L^{2}\oplus JL^{2}}\,.

(C.65)

Proof of
Lemma

C.5
.

Let
\psi\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
. Since
K_{1}^{\infty}\psi=V\ast\psi
and
K_{2}^{\infty}J\psi=V\ast\psi^{*}{\mathrm{e}}^{-2{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)}
one can verify via Fourier transform that

\displaystyle\|[y^{2},K_{1}^{\infty}]\psi\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|(y^{2}+1)V\|_{1}\|(y^{2}+1)^{1/2}\psi\|_{2}\,,

(C.66)

\displaystyle\|[y^{2},K_{2}^{\infty}J]\psi\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|(y^{2}+1)V\|_{1}\|(y^{2}+1)^{1/2}\psi\|_{2}\,,

(C.67)

\displaystyle\|[y^{2},-\Delta]\psi\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|(1+y\nabla)\psi\|_{2}\overset{\eqref{eq:aux-eq-67}}{\leq}C\|h\psi\|_{2}\,,

(C.68)

\displaystyle\|hK_{2}^{\infty}\psi\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|(1+y^{2})V\|_{1}\|h\psi\|_{2}\,.

(C.69)

Since
{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}
is translation-invariant it commutes with the Laplacian. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left[h\oplus JhJ^{*},{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}\right]{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\left[y^{2}\oplus Jy^{2}J^{*},{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}\right]{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|(h\oplus JhJ^{*}){\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\right\|\,.

We use
({\mathcal{A}}^{\infty})^{*}=S{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}S
to conclude

\displaystyle\pm\partial_{t}\|(h\oplus JhJ^{*}){\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\|^{2}=\mp\mathrm{Im}\Big\{{\left\langle(h\oplus JhJ^{*})^{2}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F,{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\ -{\left\langle{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F,(h\oplus JhJ^{*})^{2}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\right\rangle}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\mp\mathrm{Im}\Big\{{\left\langle{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F,\big[(h\oplus JhJ^{*})^{2},{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}\big]{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\right\rangle}

(C.70)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\quad+{\left\langle{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F,\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}-S{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}S\big)(h\oplus JhJ^{*})^{2}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\right\rangle}\Big\}\,.

(C.71)

First, we estimate (
C.70
) using
({\mathcal{A}}^{\infty})^{*}=S{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}S
:

(
C.70
)

\displaystyle\leq\|h\oplus JhJ^{*}\cdot{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\|\left\|\left[h\oplus JhJ^{*},{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}\right]{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|S\left[h\oplus JhJ^{*},{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}\right]S{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\right\|\|(h\oplus JhJ^{*}){\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|(h\oplus JhJ^{*}){\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\right\|^{2}\,.

The second term (
C.71
) is controlled in the same way using (
C.69
)

(
C.71
)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(h\oplus JhJ^{*})\begin{pmatrix}0&-2K_{2}^{\infty}\\
2(K_{2}^{\infty})^{*}&0\end{pmatrix}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\right\|\|(h\oplus JhJ^{*}){\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\|

\displaystyle\leq\|(h\oplus JhJ^{*}){\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\|^{2}\,.

The claim follows from Grönwall’s Lemma and the estimates above. Moreover, since
({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}=({\mathcal{V}}_{-t}^{\infty})
(see (
5.13
)), the bound (
C.65
) also holds for the inverse dynamics. Consequently,
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}\big(D(h)\oplus JD(h)\big)=D(h)\oplus JD(h)
. ∎

Lemma

C.3
and
Lemma

C.5
allow us to conclude the convergence of
{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)S}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}
to
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}
.

Lemma C.6
.

Assume the conditions of
Lemma

C.3
and set
h=-\Delta+y^{2}+1
. Then for all
T\geq 0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
\Lambda\geq 1
,
F\in D(h)\oplus JD(h)
and
-T\leq t\leq T

\displaystyle\left\|\left({\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)S}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}-{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}\right)F\right\|+\left\|\left(({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)S}-({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}\right)F\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma}\big\|\big(h\oplus JhJ^{*}\big)F\big\|\,.

(C.72)

Proof of
Lemma

C.6
.

Recall that the Bogoliubov map
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}
satisfies the evolution equation
{\mathrm{i}}\partial_{t}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}={\mathcal{A}}^{\Lambda}(t){\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}
,
{\mathcal{V}}_{0}^{\Lambda}=I
(see (
1.21
)).
We remark that

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{{\mathcal{L}}(L^{2},L^{2})}\leq C\,.

(C.73)

To prove this, write

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}=:\begin{pmatrix}U_{t}^{\Lambda}&J^{*}V_{t}^{\Lambda}J^{*}\\
V_{t}^{\Lambda}&JU_{t}^{\Lambda}J^{*}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(C.74)

Then (similar to
[
7
, Lemma 4.9]
) it follows from a Grönwall argument with the use of Duhamel and the time evolution of
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}
in (
1.21
) that

\displaystyle\|U_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\text{op}}+\|V_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\text{op}}\leq 1+\int_{0}^{t}\|K_{2}^{\Lambda}(\tau)\|_{\rm op}\big(\|U_{\tau}^{\Lambda}\|_{\rm op}+\|V_{\tau}^{\Lambda}\|_{\rm op}\big)d\tau\,.

Since
\|K_{2}^{\Lambda}(\tau)\|_{\rm op}\leq C
it follows immediately that
\|{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\rm op}\leq C
. Indeed, the bound on
K_{2}
follows directly from its definition (
1.16
):

\displaystyle\|K_{2}^{\Lambda}(t)J\psi\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|Q_{t}^{\Lambda}\tilde{K}_{2}^{\Lambda}(t)JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}\psi\|_{2}\leq\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}V\ast(\varphi_{t}(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}\psi)^{*})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\|V\|_{2}\|\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\|Q_{t}^{\Lambda}\psi\|_{2}\leq C\|\psi\|_{2}\,.

(C.75)

Next, let
F\in D(h)\oplus JD(h)
and set
\nu=\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)
.
Then

\displaystyle\pm\partial_{t}\|(1-({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\nu S}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})F\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\pm 2\mathrm{Im}\big\langle(1-({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\nu S}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})F,({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}({\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\nu S}{\mathcal{A}}^{\Lambda}(t){\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\nu S}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-\nu S-{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}){\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\|(1-({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\nu S}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})F\|^{2}

(C.76)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|({\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\nu S}{\mathcal{A}}^{\Lambda}(t){\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\nu S}-\nu S-{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}){\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\|^{2}\,.

(C.77)

The second term (
C.77
) can be estimated using
Lemma

C.3
together with the definitions (
1.22
) and (
5.6
) of
{\mathcal{A}}^{\Lambda}(s)
and
{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}
:

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:aux-eq-103}\leq C\Lambda^{-2\gamma}\|\big((1+y^{2})\oplus J(1+y^{2})J\big){\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}F\|^{2}\,.

(C.78)

Since
(1+y^{2})\leq h
and the uniform boundedness of
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}
with respect to the graph norm of
h\oplus JhJ^{*}
(see
Lemma

C.5
), it follows that

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:aux-eq-143}\leq C\Lambda^{-2\gamma}\|\big(h\oplus JhJ^{*}\big)F\|^{2}\,.

(C.79)

Thus by Grönwall’s Lemma using the estimates (
C.76
) and (
C.79
), we have for all
F\in D(h)\oplus JD(h)

\displaystyle\|(1-({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\nu S}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})F\|\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma}\|\big(h\oplus JhJ^{*}\big)F\|\,.

(C.80)

The claim then follows from the uniform boundedness of
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}
with respect to
h\oplus JhJ^{*}
, and the fact that
({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}={\mathcal{V}}_{-t}^{\infty}
.
∎

From
Lemma

C.6
we conclude strong convergence of
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}
on the whole
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\oplus JL^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
.

Corollary C.7
.

Assume the conditions of
Lemma

C.3
.
Then we have in the limit
\Lambda\to\infty
that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)S}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}

\displaystyle\to{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}\,,

(C.81)

\displaystyle({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\left(\int V-\mu^{\infty}\right)S}

\displaystyle\to({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}

(C.82)

strongly as operators on
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\oplus JL^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
.

The following lemma describes the convergence of
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}
when applied to the state appearing in the creation and annihilation operator of the finite-volume Bogoliubov–Fröhlich Hamiltonian (see (
1.17
)).

Lemma C.8
.

Assume the conditions of
Lemma

C.3
and let
\gamma
be given by (
C.47
).

i)

Let
\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{3}
and assume
2.4
|β|
. Then we have that there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\Big\|\Big(({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}(\partial^{\beta}W_{x})\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}(\partial^{\beta}W_{x})\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}\left(\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\oplus J\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\right)\Big)\Big\|_{L^{2}\oplus JL^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma/4}\Big(\|(1+y^{2}-\Delta)^{1/4}\partial^{\beta}W\|_{2}+\|\partial^{\beta}W\|_{\infty}\Big)\,.

(C.83)

Assume
2.4
2
and
2.7
4
. Then we have that there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all volumes
\Lambda\geq 1

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\Big\|\ \partial_{t}\Big(({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}\left(W_{x}\oplus JW_{x}\right)\Big)\Big\|_{L^{2}\oplus JL^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma/4}\sum_{|\beta|\leq 2}\Big(\|(1+y^{2}-\Delta)^{1/4}\partial^{\beta}W\|_{2}+\|\partial^{\beta}W\|_{\infty}\Big)\,.

(C.84)

Proof.

Let
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
be fixed. We start with the proof of (
C.83
). We have

\displaystyle(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\Big\|({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}\nu S}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}\nu S}\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}(\partial^{\beta}W_{x})\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}(\partial^{\beta}W_{x})\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}\left(\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\oplus J\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\right)\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\Big\|\left(({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}\nu S}-({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}\right)\left(\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\oplus J\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\right)\Big\|

(C.85)

\displaystyle+C(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\Big\|{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}\nu S}\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}(\partial^{\beta}W_{x})\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}(\partial^{\beta}W_{x})\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\oplus J\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\Big\|\,.

(C.86)

The first term is estimated with
Lemma

C.6
and the Heinz-Kato interpolation Theorem
[
36
, Chapter IX, Proposition 9]

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:aux-eq-114}\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma/4}(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\big\|\big(h\oplus JhJ^{*}\big)^{1/4}\left(\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\oplus J\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\right)\big\|\,,

(C.87)

where
h=1+y^{2}-\Delta
. Similarly, for the second term, in analogy with
Lemma

C.3
, using the Heinz-Kato Theorem, we get

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:aux-eq-115}\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma/4}\left((1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\big\|(1+y^{2})^{1/4}\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\big\|_{2}+\|\partial^{\beta}W\|_{\infty}\right)\,.

(C.88)

Using
W_{x}(y)=W(x-y)
and a simple substitution argument, we get
(1+x^{2})^{-1}\|h\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\|_{2}\leq C\|h\partial^{\beta}W\|_{2}
. Then by
(1+x^{2})^{-1}\|h\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\|_{2}=(1+x^{2})^{-1}\|T_{x}^{*}hT_{x}\partial^{\beta}W\|_{2}
, where
T_{x}
denotes the translation operator by
x
, and the Heinz-Kato interpolation Theorem

\displaystyle(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\|h^{1/4}\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\|_{2}\leq C\|h^{1/4}\partial^{\beta}W\|_{2}\,,

(C.89)

which gives an
x
-independent estimate.
Combining (
C.87
), (
C.88
) and (
C.89
) proves (
C.83
). Note that the constants
C
appearing above are all
x
-independent.

Next, we prove (
C.84
). Using the evolution equations
{\mathrm{i}}\partial_{t}({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}F=({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}(-{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty})F
and
{\mathrm{i}}\partial_{t}({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}F=({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}(-{\mathcal{A}}^{\Lambda}(t))F
, we have

\displaystyle(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\Big\|\partial_{t}\Big(({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}\left(W_{x}\oplus JW_{x}\right)\Big)\Big\|_{L^{2}\oplus JL^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\Big\|({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}\big(-{\mathcal{A}}^{\Lambda}(t)\big)\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}\big(-{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}-\nu S\big)\left(W_{x}\oplus JW_{x}\right)\Big\|

(C.90)

\displaystyle\quad+(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\Big\|({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}{\mathrm{i}}\partial_{t}\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}\nu S\left(W_{x}\oplus JW_{x}\right)\Big\|\,.

(C.91)

The first term, (
C.90
), is bounded using
\|{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda}\|_{\rm op}\leq C
(see (
C.73
)):

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:V-derivative-approx}\leq(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\big\|\big(({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}{\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\nu S}-({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}\big)\big(-{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}-\nu S\big)

\displaystyle\times\left(W_{x}\oplus JW_{x}\right)\big\|

(C.92)

\displaystyle+C(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\Big\|\Big({\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\nu S}{\mathcal{A}}^{\Lambda}(t){\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\nu S}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\nu S}\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\ -\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}+\nu S\big)\left(W_{x}\oplus JW_{x}\right)\Big)\Big\|\,,

(C.93)

where (
C.92
) is estimated with the help of
Lemma

C.6
and the Heinz-Kato Theorem

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:aux-eq-118}\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma/4}(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\big\|(h\oplus JhJ^{*})^{1/4}\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}+\nu S\big)\left(W_{x}\oplus JW_{x}\right)\big\|\,.

(C.94)

We see from the definition of
{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}
that
{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}(W_{x}\oplus JW_{x})=-\Delta W_{x}\oplus J\Delta W_{x}
, where the
K_{1}^{\infty}
and
K_{2}^{\infty}
contributions cancel, since
W_{x}
is real valued. Using this and an argument similar to (
C.89
), we get

(
C.94
)

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma/4}\big\|(h\oplus JhJ^{*})^{1/4}(-\Delta+\nu)W_{x}\oplus J(\Delta-\nu)W_{x}\big\|

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma/4}\big(\|h^{1/4}(-\Delta)W\|_{2}+\|h^{1/4}W\|_{2}\big)\,.

(C.95)

We continue with the (
C.93
) estimate

(
C.93
)

\displaystyle\leq(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\Big\|\big({\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\nu S}{\mathcal{A}}^{\Lambda}(t){\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\nu S}-{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty}-\nu S\big)\left(W_{x}\oplus JW_{x}\right)\Big\|

(C.96)

\displaystyle\quad+(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\Big\|{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\nu S}{\mathcal{A}}^{\Lambda}(t){\mathrm{e}}^{-{\mathrm{i}}t\nu S}\Big(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\nu}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\nu}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-W_{x}\oplus JW_{x}\Big)\Big\|\,.

(C.97)

The bound for (
C.96
) follows from
Lemma

C.3
, the Heinz-Kato Theorem, and (
C.89
)

(
C.96
)

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma/4}(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\big\|\big((1+y^{2})\oplus(1+y^{2})\big)^{1/4}(W_{x}\oplus JW_{x})\big\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|(1+y^{2})^{1/4}W\|_{2}\,.

(C.98)

For the last term, (
C.97
), using the fact that all operators, except the Laplacian, appearing in the definition of
{\mathcal{A}}^{\Lambda}(t)
(see (
1.22
)) are uniformly bounded in
\Lambda
, we first observe that

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:aux-eq-122}\leq C(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\left\|(-\Delta+1)\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\nu}-W_{x}\right)\right\|\,.

(C.99)

By separating the terms where all derivatives act on
W_{x}
from those where they act on
\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}
or
Q_{t}^{\Lambda}
, we get

(
C.99
)

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{|\beta|\in\{0,2\}}(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\Big\|Q_{t}^{\Lambda}(\partial^{\beta}W_{x})\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}{\mathrm{e}}^{{\mathrm{i}}t\nu}-\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\Big\|

\displaystyle\quad+C\Lambda^{-1/3}\sum_{|\beta|\leq 1}(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\Big\|\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\Big\|_{2}\,.

(C.100)

Applying
Lemma

C.3
together with the Heinz-Kato Theorem and again using (
C.89
), yields

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:aux-eq-124}\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma/4}\sum_{|\beta|\leq 2}\big\|(1+y^{2})^{1/4}\partial^{\beta}W\big\|_{2}+C\Lambda^{-1/6}\sum_{|\beta|\leq 2}\|\partial^{\beta}W\|_{\infty}\,.

(C.101)

Collecting all contributions, we conclude

(
C.90
)

\displaystyle\leq\eqref{eq:aux-eq-118}+\eqref{eq:aux-eq-119}\leq\eqref{eq:aux-eq-69}+\eqref{eq:aux-eq-121}+\eqref{eq:aux-eq-122}

\displaystyle\leq\eqref{eq:aux-eq-69}+\eqref{eq:aux-eq-123}+\eqref{eq:aux-eq-125}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma/4}\sum_{|\beta|\leq 2}\left(\|h^{1/4}\partial^{\beta}W\|_{2}+\|\partial^{\beta}W\|_{\infty}\right)\,.

(C.102)

The second estimate required for (
C.84
) concerns (
C.91
), namely, the case where the time derivative acts on the state.
This bound follows by a straightforward application of
Lemma

C.3
and
Lemma

C.6
, together with the Heinz–Kato theorem:

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:state-derivative-approx}\leq C\Lambda^{-\gamma/4}\big(\|h^{1/4}W\|_{2}+\|W\|_{\infty}\big)\,.

(C.103)

Combining (
C.102
) with (
C.103
), we conclude the claimed convergence rate in (
C.84
).
∎

We now use the convergence established in
Lemma

C.8
to prove uniform boundedness with respect to both
x
and
\Lambda
. In fact, these uniform bounds remain valid even after multiplying with a Bogoliubov map
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}
. The estimates provided in the following lemma play a crucial role in the tracer localization argument (see
Lemma

4.5
).

Lemma C.9
.

Let
\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{3}
and
\gamma
be given by (
C.47
).
Assume the conditions of
Lemma

C.3
,
2.7
4
and
2.4
max{|β|,2}
.
For all densities
\rho\geq 1
and volumes
\Lambda\geq 1
let
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{L}}(L^{2}\oplus JL^{2})
be a unitarily implementable Bogoliubov map such that
\exists C>0
and
0<\epsilon\leq\gamma/4
with
\forall\Lambda,\rho\geq 1

\displaystyle\|\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}{\mathcal{T}}^{-1}(\tau\oplus J\tau J^{*})\|_{{\mathcal{L}}(L^{2}\oplus JL^{2})}\leq C\,,\quad\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{{\mathcal{L}}(L^{2}\oplus JL^{2})}\leq C\Lambda^{\epsilon}\,.

(C.104)

Then for all times
T\geq 0
, there exists a
C>0
such that for all
\rho,\Lambda\geq 1
,
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
and
-T\leq t\leq T

\displaystyle(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\left\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}(\partial^{\beta}W_{x})\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}(\partial^{\beta}W_{x})\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}\oplus JL^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\,,

(C.105)

\displaystyle(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\left\|\partial_{t}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\right)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}\oplus JL^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\,.

(C.106)

Proof.

We begin with the proof of (
C.105
). We have

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:uniform-bound-Z-Lambda-applied-to-state}\leq\left\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}(\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\oplus J\partial^{\beta}W_{x})\right\|

(C.107)

\displaystyle+(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{\rm op}\Big\|({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}(\partial^{\beta}W_{x})\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}(\partial^{\beta}W_{x})\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}(\partial^{\beta}W_{x}\oplus J\partial^{\beta}W_{x})\Big\|\,.

(C.108)

We first estimate (
C.107
). For this, we prove for
f\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})

\displaystyle\|\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}\widehat{({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}}(f\oplus J{\mathcal{C}}Rf)\|_{L^{2}\oplus JL^{2}}\leq C\|f\|_{2}\,.

(C.109)

Recall the explicit form of the Bogoliubov map
{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}
in (
5.13
):

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty}}=\begin{pmatrix}L(t)&M(t)^{*}{\mathcal{C}}RJ^{*}\\
J{\mathcal{C}}RM(t)&JL(t)J^{*}\end{pmatrix}\,,

(C.110)

\displaystyle L(t)=\cos(\omega t)-{\mathrm{i}}\frac{\tfrac{p^{2}}{2}+(2\pi)^{3/2}\widehat{V}}{\omega}\sin(\omega t)\,,\quad M(t)=-{\mathrm{i}}\frac{(2\pi)^{3/2}\widehat{V}}{\omega}\sin(\omega t)\,.

We remark that from (
C.104
), we can conclude

\displaystyle\|\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}(f\oplus J{\mathcal{C}}Rf)\|_{{\mathcal{L}}(L^{2}\oplus JL^{2})}\leq\|\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}{\mathcal{T}}^{-1}(\tau\oplus J\tau J^{*})\|\|(f\oplus J{\mathcal{C}}Rf)\|

(C.111)

by inserting
{\mathcal{T}}^{-1}{\mathcal{T}}
.
Now, since
\widehat{({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}}=\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}_{-t}^{\infty}}
, using (
C.111
) and (
C.104
), we get

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:Z-Lambda-V-infty-bound}=\left\|\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}\begin{pmatrix}\big(L(-t)+M^{*}(-t)\big)f\\
J{\mathcal{C}}R\big(M(-t)+L^{*}(-t)\big)f\end{pmatrix}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}\begin{pmatrix}\cos(\omega t)f\\
J{\mathcal{C}}R\cos(\omega t)f\end{pmatrix}\right\|+\left\|\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}\begin{pmatrix}{\mathrm{i}}\tfrac{p^{2}}{2\omega}\sin(\omega t)f\\
J{\mathcal{C}}R(-{\mathrm{i}})\tfrac{p^{2}}{2\omega}\sin(\omega t)f\end{pmatrix}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|f\right\|_{2}+C\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\tau^{-1}&\\
0&J\tau^{-1}J^{*}\end{pmatrix}{\mathcal{T}}\begin{pmatrix}{\mathrm{i}}\tfrac{p^{2}}{2\omega}\sin(\omega t)f\\
J{\mathcal{C}}R(-{\mathrm{i}})\tfrac{p^{2}}{2\omega}\sin(\omega t)f\end{pmatrix}\right\|\,.

(C.112)

Applying the definition of
{\mathcal{T}}
(see (
1.24
)) to the second term in (
C.112
), we find

\displaystyle\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\tau^{-1}&\\
0&J\tau^{-1}J^{*}\end{pmatrix}{\mathcal{T}}\begin{pmatrix}{\mathrm{i}}\tfrac{p^{2}}{2\omega}\sin(\omega t)f\\
J{\mathcal{C}}R(-{\mathrm{i}})\tfrac{p^{2}}{2\omega}\sin(\omega t)f\end{pmatrix}\right\|

\displaystyle=C\|\tau^{-2}\tfrac{p^{2}}{2\omega}\sin(\omega t)f\|_{2}\leq C\|f\|_{2}\,.

(C.113)

From (
C.112
) and (
C.113
) we conclude (
C.109
), and hence obtain the bound
\eqref{eq:aux-eq-127}\leq C
. Together with the estimate
\eqref{eq:aux-eq-128}\leq C
, obtained using
Lemma

C.8
, this yields (
C.105
).

The estimate of (
C.106
) follows analogously using
Lemma

C.8
, (
C.104
) and
\epsilon\leq\gamma/4
:

\displaystyle(1+x^{2})^{-1/4}\left\|\partial_{t}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\Lambda})^{-1}\left(Q_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\oplus JQ_{t}^{\Lambda}W_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\Lambda}\right)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}\oplus JL^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{\epsilon-\gamma/4}+\left\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}(-{\mathcal{A}}^{\infty})(W_{x}\oplus JW_{x})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C+\left\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}({\mathcal{V}}_{t}^{\infty})^{-1}\big(\tfrac{p^{2}}{2}W_{x}\oplus J-\tfrac{p^{2}}{2}W_{x}\big)\right\|

(C.114)

The second term in (
C.114
) can now be estimated using (
C.110
), (
C.104
) and (
C.111
):

(
C.114
)

\displaystyle\leq C+C\left\|\tau^{-2}p^{2}\cos(\omega t)W_{x}\right\|+C\left\|{\mathrm{i}}\tfrac{p^{2}}{2\omega}\left(\tfrac{p^{2}}{2}+(2\pi)^{3/2}\widehat{V}\right)\sin(\omega t)W_{x}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C+C\|W\|_{H^{2}}(1+|t|)(1+\|\widehat{V}\|_{\infty})\,,

(C.115)

where in the last term we estimated small and large momenta separately. Now, (
C.106
) follows from (
C.115
).
∎

Appendix D
An Explicit Example of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}

In this section, we construct a unitarily implementable Bogoliubov map
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}
to approximate
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\infty}=\widecheck{{\mathcal{T}}}
in the sense of
2.1
. For the reader’s convenience we repeat the definition of
{\mathcal{T}}
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\tau+\tau^{-1}&(\tau-\tau^{-1})R{\mathcal{C}}J^{*}\\
J{\mathcal{C}}R(\tau-\tau^{-1})&J(\tau+\tau^{-1})J^{*}\end{pmatrix}\,,

(D.1)

with
{\mathcal{C}}\psi=\psi^{*}
,
R\psi(p)=\psi(-p)
,
\tau=\frac{1}{(1+T)^{1/4}}
. The operator
T
is given by
T=|p|^{-1}(2\pi)^{3/2}\widehat{V}(p)|p|^{-1}
, where we assume
\widehat{V}(0)\geq 0
.
The main difficulty in constructing
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}
arises from the fact that, in general,
{\mathcal{T}}
is neither a bounded operator nor unitarily implementable, whereas
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}
must satisfy both properties.
To overcome this issue, we introduce an infrared cutoff by replacing
|p|^{-1}
in the definition of
T
with
(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon})^{-1/2}
for some
\epsilon>0
.
The resulting operator is bounded but still not unitarily implementable.
To ensure unitary implementability, we further replace
(2\pi)^{3/2}\widehat{V}(p)
by its finite-volume approximation

{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}:=\tilde{K}_{1}^{\Lambda}{\mathrm{e}}^{2{\mathrm{i}}t\nu}
,
\nu=\int V-\mu^{\infty}
.

The operator obtained in this way is then dressed with
\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}
so as to leave the excitation space invariant. More precisely, our approximation of
\tau
is defined by

\displaystyle\tau^{\Lambda}

\displaystyle=(1+T^{\Lambda})^{-\frac{1}{4}}\,,

(D.2)

\displaystyle T^{\Lambda}

\displaystyle=\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}\left(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon}\right)^{-1/2}\widehat{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}}\left(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon}\right)^{-1/2}\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}\,.

(D.3)

And we set

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}=\begin{pmatrix}\tau^{\Lambda}+(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}&\left(\tau^{\Lambda}-(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}\right){\mathcal{C}}RJ^{*}\\
J\left(\tau^{\Lambda}-(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}\right){\mathcal{C}}R&J\left(\tau^{\Lambda}+(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}\right)J^{*}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(D.4)

In order for
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}
to define a Bogoliubov map, it is necessary to assume that the initial condensate is real-valued.
In the following, we verify that this choice of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}
satisfies
2.1
(see
Lemma

D.4
for a precise statement).

Remark D.1
.

On the Torus, an infrared regularization of the operator diagonalizing the Bogoliubov dynamics is not necessary, as the ground state of the Laplacian (corresponding to the condensate) is separated by a gap scaling like
\Lambda^{-2/3}
, which naturally provides an infrared cutoff. The cutoff introduced here,
\Lambda^{-\epsilon}
with
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, would then correspond to allowing only excitations with momenta that are not too small in the initial data.

The following lemma allows us to control the difference of
\tau^{\Lambda}
and
\tau
coming from the regularized momentum terms
(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon})^{-1/2}
and
\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}
.

Lemma D.2
.

Let
\widehat{V}\geq 0
and assume
2.6
.

i)

Then
\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}D(|p|)\subset D(|p|)
,
\widehat{\tilde{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}D(|p|)\subset D(|p|)
and
\exists C>0
such that
\forall\Lambda\geq 1
and
f\in D(|p|)

\displaystyle\|\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}|p|-|p|\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}\|_{{\mathcal{L}}(L^{2})}+\|\widehat{\tilde{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}|p|-|p|\widehat{\tilde{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}\|_{{\mathcal{L}}(L^{2})}\leq C\Lambda^{-1/3}\,.

(D.5)

ii)

Then we have for
\psi\in D(|p|)
,
2/3>\epsilon>0
that

\displaystyle\Big\|\Big(\frac{1}{(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon})^{1/2}}-\frac{1}{|p|}\Big)|p|\psi\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\Lambda^{\epsilon/2}}\|\psi\|_{2}\,,

(D.6)

\displaystyle\Big\|\Big(\frac{1}{(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon})^{1/2}}\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}-\frac{1}{|p|}\Big)|p|\psi\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\to 0\,,\ \Lambda\to\infty\,.

(D.7)

Proof of
Lemma

D.2
.

For the proof of the first part let
f\in D(|p|)
. Then we have with
\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}=1-\Lambda^{-1}\widehat{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}}\big\langle\widehat{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}},\,.\,\big\rangle
and
\big\||p|\widehat{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}}\big\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\big\|p_{i}\widehat{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}}\big\|_{2}^{2}
that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}|p|-|p|\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}\right)f\right\|\leq 2\Lambda^{-1/2}\big\||p|\widehat{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}}\big\|_{2}\|f\|_{2}\leq C\Lambda^{-1/3}\|f\|_{2}\,,

(D.8)

which proves the estimate of
\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}
in (
D.5
). Now, since
\tilde{K}_{1}^{\Lambda}f=\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}V\ast((\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda})^{*}f)
we have
\widehat{\tilde{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}f=\int K(p,r)f(r)dr
with
K(p,r)=(2\pi)^{-3/2}\int\widehat{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}}(p-q)\widehat{V}(q)\widehat{(\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda})^{*}}(q-r)dq
. Then

\displaystyle\|\big(\widehat{\tilde{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}|p|-|p|\widehat{\tilde{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}\big)f\|_{2}^{2}=\int dp\left|\int dr(|r|-|p|)K(p,r)f(r)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int dp\left(\int dr\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{|r_{i}-p_{i}|}{(2\pi)^{3/2}}\left|\int dq\widehat{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}}(p-q)\widehat{V}(q)\widehat{(\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda})^{*}}(q-r)f(r)\right|\right)^{2}\,.

(D.9)

Splitting
|r_{i}-p_{i}|\leq|r_{i}-q_{i}|+|q_{i}-p_{i}|
gives

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:aux-eq-138}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Big\{\left\||\widehat{\partial_{i}\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}}|\ast\left(|\widehat{V}|\cdot|\widehat{(\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda})^{*}}|\ast|f|\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\||\widehat{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}}|\ast\left(|\widehat{V}|\cdot|\widehat{(\partial_{i}\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda})^{*}}|\ast|f|\right)\right\|_{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{3}\|\widehat{\partial_{i}\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}}\|_{1}\|\widehat{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}}\|_{1}\|\widehat{V}\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{2}\,,

which proves (
D.5
) using
2.6
.

The second part follows from

\displaystyle\Big\|\big((p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon})^{-1/2}\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}-|p|^{-1}\big)|p|\psi\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon})^{-1/2}\big[\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}},|p|\big]\psi\right\|+\Big\|\big((p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon})^{-1/2}|p|\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}-1\big)\psi\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{\epsilon/2-1/3}\|\psi\|_{2}+\|((p^{2}+\Lambda^{\epsilon})^{-1/2}|p|-1)\psi\|_{2}+\|(\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}-1)\psi\|_{2}\,,

(D.10)

where we used (
D.5
) and
(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon})^{-1/2}|p|\leq 1
. We conclude the convergence
(
D.7
) from (
D.10
) and
C.4
. The estimate (
D.6
) is proven analogously.
∎

We show that, in an appropriate sense,
\tau^{\Lambda}
and its inverse converge to
\tau
and
\tau^{-1}
, respectively. In contrast to
(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}
, the limit
\tau^{-1}
is unbounded, so to obtain convergence we regularize both with
\tau^{2}
.

Lemma D.3

(Convergence of
\tau^{\Lambda}
)
.

Let
\widehat{V}\geq 0
,
\widehat{V}(0)>0
and
0<\epsilon<1/3
. Assume the conditions of
Lemma

C.3
and
2.7
2
. Then we have that

\displaystyle(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}\tau^{2}\to\tau\,,\quad\tau^{\Lambda}\to\tau\,,\quad\text{as }\Lambda\to\infty

(D.11)

strongly as operators on
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
.

Proof of
Lemma

D.3
.

We begin by proving that for
\psi\in D(|p|^{2})
one has

\displaystyle\big\|\big(T^{\Lambda}-T\big)|p|^{2}\psi\big\|_{2}\to 0\,,\ \Lambda\to\infty\,.

(D.12)

Using the definitions of
T^{\Lambda}
and
T
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big\|\big(T^{\Lambda}-T\big)|p|^{2}\psi\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}\left(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon}\right)^{-1/2}\widehat{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}}\left(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon}\right)^{-1/2}\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}-|p|^{-1}(2\pi)^{3/2}\widehat{V}|p|^{-1}\right)|p|^{2}\psi\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}-1\right)(2\pi)^{3/2}\widehat{V}\psi\right\|

(D.13)

\displaystyle+\left\|\left(\left(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon}\right)^{-1/2}\widehat{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}}\left(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon}\right)^{-1/2}\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}-|p|^{-1}(2\pi)^{3/2}\widehat{V}|p|^{-1}\right)|p|^{2}\psi\right\|\,.

(D.14)

The term in (
D.13
) converges to zero as
\Lambda\to\infty
by
C.4
.
Next, we estimate (
D.14
) by

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:aux-eq-93}\leq\left\|\left(\left(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon}\right)^{-1/2}-|p|^{-1}\right)|p|\widehat{V}\psi\right\|

(D.15)

\displaystyle+\left\|\left(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon}\right)^{-1/2}\left(\widehat{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}}\left(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon}\right)^{-1/2}\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}-(2\pi)^{3/2}\widehat{V}|p|^{-1}\right)|p|^{2}\psi\right\|\,.

(D.16)

The contribution in (
D.15
) converges to zero by
Lemma

D.2
. To treat (
D.16
), we commute
|p|
through the operator to compensate the left factor
(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon})^{-1/2}
:

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:aux-eq-95}\leq\left\|\left(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon}\right)^{-1/2}\widehat{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}}\left(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon}\right)^{-1/2}\left[\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}},|p|\right]|p|\psi\right\|

(D.17)

\displaystyle+\left\|\left(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon}\right)^{-1/2}\big[\widehat{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}},|p|\big]\left(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon}\right)^{-1/2}|p|\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}|p|\psi\right\|

(D.18)

\displaystyle+\left\|\left(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}|p|\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\left(\widehat{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}}\left(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon}\right)^{-1/2}\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}-(2\pi)^{3/2}\widehat{V}|p|^{-1}\right)|p|\psi\right\|\,.

(D.19)

By
Lemma

D.2
, the commutators satisfy
\|[\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}},|p|]\|+\|[\widehat{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}},|p|]\|\leq C\Lambda^{-1/3}

and therefore
\eqref{eq:aux-eq-96}\leq C\Lambda^{\epsilon-1/3}\to 0
and
\eqref{eq:aux-eq-98}\to 0
for
\epsilon<1/3
.
Finally, we bound (
D.19
) by

(
D.19
)

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}}\|_{\rm op}\left\|\left(\left(p^{2}+\Lambda^{-\epsilon}\right)^{-1/2}\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}-|p|^{-1}\right)|p|\psi\right\|

(D.20)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\left(\widehat{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}}-(2\pi)^{3/2}\widehat{V}\right)|p|^{-1}|p|\psi\right\|\,,

(D.21)

where
\eqref{eq:aux-eq-100}\to 0
by
Lemma

D.2
, and
\eqref{eq:aux-eq-101}\to 0
by
C.4
. Collecting all estimates, we conclude (
D.12
).

We proceed with the proof of the strong convergence of
(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}\tau^{2}
to
\tau
. Let
\psi\in D(T^{1/2})
. Then
\tau^{2}\psi\in D(T)
, and by the functional calculus we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\left((\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}-\tau^{-1}\right)\tau^{2}\psi\right\|=\left\|\left((1+T^{\Lambda})^{1/4}-(1+T)^{1/4}\right)\tau^{2}\psi\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{\sin(\pi/4)}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{-1/4}\left(\frac{1+T^{\Lambda}}{1+\lambda(1+T^{\Lambda})}-\frac{1+T}{1+\lambda(1+T)}\right)\tau^{2}\psi\,d\lambda\right\|\,.

(D.22)

Using the identity
B/(1+\lambda B)=\lambda^{-1}-\lambda^{-1}(1+\lambda B)^{-1}
, we rewrite this as

(
D.22
)

\displaystyle=C\left\|\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{-5/4}\left(\frac{1}{1+\lambda(1+T)}-\frac{1}{1+\lambda(1+T^{\Lambda})}\right)\tau^{2}\psi\,d\lambda\right\|

\displaystyle=C\left\|\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{-1/4}\frac{1}{1+\lambda(1+T^{\Lambda})}\left(T^{\Lambda}-T\right)\frac{1}{1+\lambda(1+T)}\tau^{2}\psi\,d\lambda\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda^{-1/4}}{1+\lambda}\left\|\left(T^{\Lambda}-T\right)\frac{1}{1+\lambda(1+T)}\tau^{2}\psi\right\|d\lambda\,.

(D.23)

We prove that (
D.23
) converges to zero as
\Lambda\to\infty
by the dominated convergence theorem. First, we show pointwise convergence of the integrand. In fact,

\displaystyle\left\|\left(T^{\Lambda}-T\right)\frac{1}{1+\lambda(1+T)}\tau^{2}\psi\right\|=\left\|\left(T^{\Lambda}-T\right)|p|^{2}\frac{|p|^{-1}}{1+\lambda(1+T)}\frac{|p|^{-1}}{(1+T)^{1/2}}\psi\right\|

(D.24)

and we have the bounds
\|\frac{|p|^{-1}}{1+\lambda(1+T)}\|_{\rm op}\leq C(\lambda^{-1/2}+1)
and
\|\frac{|p|^{-1}}{(1+T)^{1/2}}\|_{\rm op}\leq C
. This follows since for small momenta
p
we have the bound
1/(p^{2}+\lambda\widehat{V}(p))^{1/2}\leq 2/(\lambda\widehat{V}(0))^{1/2}
, which holds since
\widehat{V}(0)>0
and
\widehat{V}
is continuous. The representation in (
D.24
), together with the previously established convergence of
T^{\Lambda}
to
T
in (
D.12
), implies that the integrand converges to zero.
Moreover, by an argument analogous to the proof of the convergence of
T^{\Lambda}
to
T
, one shows that
\|T^{\Lambda}|p|^{2}\|_{\rm op}+\|T|p|^{2}\|_{\rm op}\leq C
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{-1/4}}{1+\lambda}\left\|\left(T^{\Lambda}-T\right)\frac{1}{1+\lambda(1+T)}\tau^{2}\psi\right\|\leq C\frac{\lambda^{-1/4}(\lambda^{-1/2}+1)}{1+\lambda}\in L^{1}((0,\infty)_{\lambda},{\mathbb{R}})\,,

(D.25)

which provides an integrable dominating function. The dominated convergence theorem and (
D.23
) therefore yield

\|\left((\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}-\tau^{-1}\right)\tau^{2}\psi\|\to 0
for all
\psi\in D(T^{1/2})
. To extend this convergence to the whole space
L^{2}
, it remains to show that
(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}\tau^{2}
is uniformly bounded.
By an argument analogous to that used in the proof of (
D.12
), one obtains for all
\phi\in L^{2}

\displaystyle\|(1+T^{\Lambda})\frac{1}{(1+T)^{2}}\phi\|\leq C\|\phi\|

(D.26)

and thus
\|(1+T^{\Lambda})\phi\|\leq C\|(1+T)^{2}\phi\|
.
By operator monotonicity of the fourth root, this implies the uniform bound
\|(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}\tau^{2}\|_{\rm op}\leq C
.
Combining this with the bound
\|\tau\|_{\rm op}\leq 1
, we conclude that
\|\left((\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}-\tau^{-1}\right)\tau^{2}\psi\|\to 0
for all
\psi\in L^{2}
.

Next, for
\psi\in L^{2}
we compute

\displaystyle\|(\tau^{\Lambda}-\tau)\tau\psi\|=\|\tau^{\Lambda}\left(\tau^{-1}-(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}\right)\tau^{2}\psi\|\leq\|\left(\tau^{-1}-(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}\right)\tau^{2}\psi\|\to 0\,.

Since
\tau
is self-adjoint and injective, its range is dense in
L^{2}
. Together with the uniform bound
\|\tau^{\Lambda}\|_{\rm op}+\|\tau\|_{\rm op}\leq 2
, this implies that
\tau^{\Lambda}
converges strongly to
\tau
on all of
L^{2}
.

∎

The following lemma shows that
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}
defined in (
D.4
) satisfies
2.1
.

Lemma D.4
.

Let
\epsilon>0
. Let
\widehat{V}\geq 0
,
\widehat{V}(0)>0
and
0<\epsilon<1/3
. Assume the conditions of
Lemma

C.3
and
2.7
2
. Assume
\varphi_{0}(y)\in{\mathbb{R}}
for all
y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
.
Then
\tau^{\Lambda}
commutes with
{\mathcal{C}}R
, and
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}
is a unitarily implementable Bogoliubov map with

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{\epsilon/4}\,,

(D.27)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}({\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda})^{*}-1\|_{\rm HS}

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda^{1/2+\epsilon}\,.

(D.28)

For all
f,g\in L^{2}
we have the following convergence

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}{\mathcal{T}}^{-1}\left(\tau f\oplus J\tau g\right)\to\tau f\oplus J\tau g\,.

(D.29)

The commutator of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}
with translations
T_{x}
converges to zero:

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{(1+x^{2})^{1/2}}\Big\|\left[{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda},T_{x}\oplus JT_{x}J^{*}\right]F\Big\|_{2}\to 0\,,\quad\text{as }\Lambda\to\infty

(D.30)

for all
F\in L^{2}\oplus JL^{2}
.
For the corresponding unitary we have the following invariances

\displaystyle U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}{\mathcal{F}}\left(\{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\}^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle\subset{\mathcal{F}}\left(\{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\}^{\perp}\right)\,,

(D.31)

\displaystyle U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}Q({\mathrm{d}}\Gamma(-\Delta+1))

\displaystyle\subset Q({\mathrm{d}}\Gamma(-\Delta+1))\,.

(D.32)

Proof of
Lemma

D.4
.

Since
\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}(y)\in\mathbb{R}
, for all
y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
,
\tau^{\Lambda}
commutes with
\mathcal{C}R
, and hence
\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{\Lambda}
is a Bogoliubov map.
Implementability follows from the Hilbert–Schmidt bound (
D.28
), which we now verify. Using the functional calculus and an orthonormal basis
\{e_{k}\}
of
L^{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\big(\tau^{\Lambda}-(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}\big)e_{k}\|^{2}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\big\|\frac{1}{(1+T^{\Lambda})^{1/4}}\big(1-(1+T^{\Lambda})^{1/2}\big)e_{k}\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big\|\frac{1}{(1+T^{\Lambda})^{1/4}}\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{T^{\Lambda}}{(1+tT^{\Lambda})^{1/2}}e_{k}dt\Big\|^{2}\leq 1/2\|T^{\Lambda}\|_{\rm HS}\,.

(D.33)

By the definition of
T^{\Lambda}
in (
D.3
) and (
1.15
),

\displaystyle\|T^{\Lambda}\|_{\rm HS}\leq\Lambda^{\epsilon}\big\|\widehat{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}}\big\|_{\rm HS}\leq C\Lambda^{1/2+\epsilon}\,,

and thus
\|\tau^{\Lambda}-(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}\|_{\rm HS}\leq C\Lambda^{1/2+\epsilon}
, proving (
D.28
). Moreover, since
\big\|\widehat{{\mathcal{K}}_{1}^{\Lambda}}\big\|_{\rm op}\leq C
, we have
\|(1+T^{\Lambda})\|_{\rm op}\leq C\Lambda^{\epsilon}
. Taking the fourth root gives us
\|(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}\|_{\rm op}\leq\Lambda^{\epsilon/4}
, yielding (
D.27
).

Next, we prove (
D.31
). Since
T^{\Lambda}\widehat{P_{0}^{\Lambda}}=0
, where
P_{0}^{\Lambda}=1/\Lambda|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\rangle\langle\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}|
, it is easy to see that
(\tau^{\Lambda})^{\pm 1}\widehat{P_{0}^{\Lambda}}=\widehat{P_{0}^{\Lambda}}
. Thus

\displaystyle a(\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda})U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}\Omega=U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}a\big(\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\big)\Omega=0\,,

(D.34)

so
U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}\Omega\in{\mathcal{F}}(\{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\}^{\perp})
. Now (
D.31
) follows from
[(\tau^{\Lambda})^{\pm 1},\widehat{Q_{0}^{\Lambda}}]=0
, the density of
\bigcup_{n=0}^{\infty}\{A(F_{1})\cdot A(F_{n})\Omega\,|\,F_{j}\in\{\varphi_{0}\}^{\perp}\oplus J\{\varphi_{0}\}^{\perp}\}
in
{\mathcal{F}}(\{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\}^{\perp})
and the continuity of
U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}
in
{\mathcal{F}}(L^{2})
.

Next, we discuss (
D.32
). For an orthonormal basis
\{e_{k}\}\subset H^{1}
, we compute using (
1.19
)

\displaystyle{\left\langle U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}\psi,{\mathrm{d}}\Gamma(-\Delta)U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}\psi\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k}\|a((-\Delta)^{1/2}e_{k})U_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}^{*}\psi\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k}\|A\big({\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}((-\Delta)^{1/2}e_{k}\oplus J0)\big)\psi\|^{2}\,.

(D.35)

Using the definition of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}
, (
D.4
), this splits into the diagonal term (
D.36
) and the off-diagonal term (
D.37
):

(
D.35
)

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{k}\|a\big({\mathcal{F}}^{-1}(\tau^{\Lambda}+(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1})|p|\widehat{e_{k}}\big)\psi\|^{2}

(D.36)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k}\|a^{*}\big({\mathcal{F}}^{-1}(\tau^{\Lambda}-(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}){\mathcal{C}}R|p|\widehat{e_{k}}\big)\psi\|^{2}\,.

(D.37)

Since
(\tau^{\Lambda}-(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1})|p|
is Hilbert-Schmidt, which follows analogously to (
D.33
) using the commutator bound (
D.5
), the second term is bounded by

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:aux-eq-134}\leq\|(\tau^{\Lambda}-(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1})|p|\|_{\rm HS}\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)\psi\|<\infty\,.

(D.38)

The first term (
D.36
) can be rewritten as

(
D.36
)

\displaystyle={\left\langle\widehat{\psi},{\mathrm{d}}\Gamma\big((\tau^{\Lambda}+(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1})|p|^{2}(\tau^{\Lambda}+(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1})\big)\widehat{\psi}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left\||p|_{k}(\tau^{\Lambda}+(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1})_{k}\psi^{(n)}\right\|_{2}^{2}\,.

(D.39)

But with the integral representations for
0<\alpha<1
,
\phi\in{\mathscr{H}}

\displaystyle(1+T^{\Lambda})^{\alpha}\phi

\displaystyle=\frac{\sin(\alpha\pi)}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{-\alpha}(1+T^{\Lambda})}{1+t(1+T^{\Lambda})}\phi\,dt\,,

(D.40)

\displaystyle(1+T^{\Lambda})^{-\alpha}\phi

\displaystyle=\frac{\sin(\alpha\pi)}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{-\alpha}}{t+(1+T^{\Lambda})}\phi\,dt

(D.41)

and
Lemma

D.2
it is easily shown that
\||p|(\tau^{\Lambda})^{\pm 1}\phi\|_{2}\leq C_{\Lambda}\|\phi\|+C\||p|\phi\|
for all
\phi\in H^{1}
, and thus

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:aux-eq-135}\leq C_{\Lambda}\|({\mathcal{N}}+1)^{1/2}\psi\|^{2}+C{\left\langle\psi,{\mathrm{d}}\Gamma(-\Delta)\psi\right\rangle}<\infty\,.

(D.42)

Now, we conclude (
D.32
) from (
D.38
) and (
D.42
).

Next we prove the convergence of the commutator of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}
with translation
T_{x}
in (
D.30
). We start by showing that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
and
f\in L^{2}
:

\displaystyle\big\|\big[Q_{0}^{\Lambda},T_{x}\big]f\big\|_{2}\leq\Lambda^{-1/3}|x|\|f\|_{2}\,,

(D.43)

\displaystyle\big\|\big[\tilde{K}_{1}^{\Lambda},T_{x}\big]f\big\|_{2}\leq\Lambda^{-1/3}|x|\|f\|_{2}\,.

(D.44)

To this end, using the definition of
Q_{0}^{\Lambda}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:Commutator-Q-0-with-translations}=\Lambda^{-1}\|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}-T_{x}\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{2}|{\left\langle\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda},T_{x}f\right\rangle}|+\Lambda^{-1}\|T_{x}\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{2}|{\left\langle\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda},(T_{x}-1)f\right\rangle}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\Lambda^{-1}\|f\|_{2}\|(T_{x}-1)\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{2}\leq 2\Lambda^{-1}\|f\|_{2}\big\|\sin(px/2)\widehat{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{-1}|x|\|f\|_{2}\big\||p|^{2}\widehat{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}}\big\|_{2}^{1/2}\big\|\widehat{\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}}\big\|_{2}^{1/2}\leq C\|f\|_{2}\Lambda^{-1/3}|x|\,,

(D.45)

which proves (
D.43
). For the commutator with
\tilde{K}_{1}^{\Lambda}(0)
we use its definition (
1.15
) and the mean-value theorem to get

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:Commutator-K-1-with-translations}=\|\tilde{K}_{1}^{\Lambda}T_{x}f-T_{x}\tilde{K}_{1}^{\Lambda}f\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}V\ast\big((T_{x}\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda})^{*}T_{x}f\big)-T_{x}\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}V\ast\big((\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda})^{*}T_{x}f\big)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|(T_{x}-1)\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\|V\|_{1}\|\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|f\|_{2}\|\nabla\varphi_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{\infty}|x|\leq C\|f\|_{2}\Lambda^{-1/3}|x|\,,

(D.46)

which proves (
D.44
).
With this at hand it is readily checked that

\displaystyle\left\|\big[T^{\Lambda},\widehat{T_{x}}\big]f\right\|\leq C\Lambda^{\epsilon-1/3}\|f\|_{2}|x|

(D.47)

and with the use of the integral formulas (
D.40
) and (
D.41
) also

\displaystyle\left\|\big[(1+T^{\Lambda})^{\pm 1/4},\widehat{T_{x}}\big]f\right\|_{2}\leq C\Lambda^{\epsilon-1/3}\|f\|_{2}|x|\,.

(D.48)

Thus it follows for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
and
F\in L^{2}\oplus JL^{2}
that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{(1+x^{2})^{1/2}}\Big\|\left[{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda},T_{x}\oplus JT_{x}J^{*}\right]F\Big\|\leq C\Lambda^{\epsilon-1/3}\|F\|

(D.49)

and then also (
D.30
).

It remains to prove the convergence of
\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}
to
{\mathcal{T}}
in the sense of (
D.29
). This follows from the identity

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}^{\Lambda}}{\mathcal{T}}^{-1}(\tau\oplus J\tau J^{*})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}\tau^{2}+\tau^{\Lambda}&\left(\tau^{\Lambda}-(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}\tau^{2}\right){\mathcal{C}}RJ^{*}\\
J{\mathcal{C}}R\left(\tau^{\Lambda}-(\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}\tau^{2}\right)&J\left((\tau^{\Lambda})^{-1}\tau^{2}+\tau^{\Lambda}\right)J^{*}\end{pmatrix}

(D.50)

together with the convergence of
\tau^{\Lambda}
to
\tau
established in
Lemma

D.3
.
∎
